     1	"""The Windows side broker of the parent owned native custody.
     2	
     3	The broker is the only creator of the native services of one round.  The
     4	parent starts it, keeps its original handle and talks to it over a private
     5	channel; the broker creates the services itself, holds their original
     6	handles and stops them again.
     7	
     8	Four properties of the broker are the reason it exists at all.
     9	
    10	* **Suspend first, adopt second.**  Every service is created with
    11	  ``CREATE_SUSPENDED``.  The broker holds the original process and thread
    12	  handle, it assigns the process to the job object of the round and only
    13	  then it resumes the thread.  A service is never started first and
    14	  adopted afterwards: a process that already ran could have broken away
    15	  from the job before the adoption.
    16	
    17	* **The job belongs to the round.**  One job object is created per round
    18	  with ``JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`` and without
    19	  ``JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK``.  The children of the services inherit
    20	  that constraint.  When the broker closes the job handle, the round is
    21	  gone.  The broker is the only owner of that handle and of the stop.
    22	
    23	* **Nothing else is inherited.**  The services get their standard output
    24	  and their standard error and the null device as their standard input.
    25	  They never get the control channel of the broker, the job handle or any
    26	  other handle of the broker.  On Windows the inheritance set is not
    27	  trusted to the default: it is passed explicitly as the handle list of
    28	  the process attributes, and every handle of that list is created as
    29	  inheritable while every other handle is not.
    30	
    31	* **Only what was created is stopped.**  The broker has no notion of a
    32	  process name, of a pid or of a record of another owner.  It stops the
    33	  handles that it holds and the job that it created, and nothing else.
    34	
    35	Nothing in this module has ever run on Windows.  Every native claim of
    36	this package therefore stays ``NOT_PROVEN``: the Windows path is written
    37	from the documented behaviour of the API and it is not a measurement.
    38	"""
    39	import collections
    40	import hashlib
    41	import json
    42	import math
    43	import os
    44	import queue
    45	import re
    46	import select
    47	import subprocess
    48	import sys
    49	import threading
    50	import time
    51	from pathlib import Path
    52	
    53	PACK_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
    54	if str(PACK_ROOT) not in sys.path:
    55	    sys.path.insert(0, str(PACK_ROOT))
    56	
    57	from tools.v0_s3_native_custody import (  # noqa: E402 - the path is set above
    58	    CHANNEL_SCHEMA, CustodyError, MAX_DESCENDANTS, MAX_FRAME_BYTES, MAX_FRAMES,
    59	    MAX_NEW_TEXT_BYTES, MAX_STREAM_BYTES, decode_command, validate_launch_plan)
    60	
    61	# The bounded limits of the broker.  They are internal limits of this
    62	# foundation; they are not a claim about any native Windows behaviour.
    63	WAIT_MILLISECONDS = 5000
    64	HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 120
    65	CLOSE_JOIN_SECONDS = 5.0
    66	READER_JOIN_SECONDS = 2.0
    67	# The ONE cleanup budget of a round.  The stop and the cleanup of a round
    68	# share ONE absolute deadline: the deadline is generated once, at the
    69	# first stop or at the first failure of the cleanup, and it is the same
    70	# value in every layer of the cleanup.  No layer adds a budget of its own
    71	# and no layer starts a new wait with a fresh timeout.
    72	CLEANUP_BUDGET_SECONDS = 5.0
    73	# The number of the startup results that the in-flight owner of a startup
    74	# step may carry.  The channel is bounded: a startup owner that produced
    75	# more than one result at a time would be an unbounded queue.
    76	STARTUP_INFLIGHT_SLOTS = 1
    77	# The bounded wait for a startup step that did not end before the
    78	# deadline of the round.  A step that is still running after this wait
    79	# keeps its owner and the round stays unknown: the round never claims a
    80	# cleanup that it cannot prove.
    81	STARTUP_SETTLE_SECONDS = 1.0
    82	MAX_PLAN_BYTES = 64 * 1024
    83	MAX_RECEIPT_BYTES = 1024 * 1024
    84	POLL_SECONDS = 0.25
    85	
    86	# The bounded waits of the private channel.  The pipe is polled in slices
    87	# of at most 50 milliseconds, so that the cancel flag and the absolute
    88	# deadline of the round are observed even while a peer is silent.
    89	POLL_SLICE_SECONDS = 0.05
    90	# The bounded wait of the **put** of the reader of the channel.  The
    91	# put is cropped to the time that is really left until the current
    92	# effective deadline of the round and it never asks for more than the
    93	# round allows.
    94	CHANNEL_QUEUE_PUT_SECONDS = 0.5
    95	PIPE_READ_BYTES = 65536
    96	# The number of the frames of the channel that is reserved for the
    97	# terminal exchange of the round.  The heartbeat may not spend them: a
    98	# round that reached its frame budget has to fail closed and it may not
    99	# reconnect and it may not renew its lease.
   100	RESERVED_TERMINAL_FRAMES = 8
   101	
   102	# The frozen environment of every child.  The variables that the existing
   103	# authorised launch wrappers remove are removed here as well, so that a
   104	# child never inherits a proxy, a user site directory or a start-up file.
   105	REMOVED_ENVIRONMENT = ('PYTHONPATH', 'PYTHONHOME', 'PYTHONSTARTUP', 'PYTHONINSPECT',
   106	                        'PYTHONUSERBASE', 'PYTHONOPTIMIZE', 'HTTP_PROXY', 'HTTPS_PROXY',
   107	                        'ALL_PROXY')
   108	
   109	#: The one lock that guards the creation of the lock of the admission of
   110	#: an endpoint.  It is a lock of the **module** and it is never the lock
   111	#: of an endpoint: two endpoints never share an admission.
   112	_ADMISSION_LOCK_INIT = threading.Lock()
   113	
   114	
   115	class BrokerError(RuntimeError):
   116	    """A refused broker step with a stable, machine readable code."""
   117	
   118	    def __init__(self, code, detail=None):
   119	        super().__init__(code)
   120	        self.code = code
   121	        self.detail = detail
   122	
   123	
   124	# -- the one context of a round ---------------------------------------------
   125	
   126	class _RoundContext:
   127	    """The ONE finite startup and cleanup context of one round.
   128	
   129	    The context holds the four things that every layer of a round has to
   130	    share and that may never be copied from one layer into the next one.
   131	
   132	    * The **clock** of the round.  Every deadline of the round is
   133	      expressed in it, so that a deadline of one domain is never
   134	      compared with a clock of another one.
   135	
   136	    * The **absolute deadline** of the round.  It is bound once and it
   137	      may only be shortened.
   138	
   139	    * The **one cleanup deadline**.  It is published **once**, by the
   140	      first caller that enters the cleanup, and every later caller
   141	      reuses that very value: two callers that entered the cleanup at the
   142	      same moment always see the same number, and the publication is one
   143	      atomic step under the lock of the context.
   144	
   145	    * The three **one way latches** of the round: the cancel, the stop
   146	      and the unknown.  A latch that was set is never cleared, and a
   147	      late event may never overwrite the unknown of the round.
   148	
   149	    Every layer of the round holds this **one object**: the core, the
   150	    loop, the transport of the channel and the endpoints of the channel.
   151	    A layer that only copied the number of a deadline would be a layer
   152	    with a deadline of its own, and two layers with two deadlines are
   153	    two budgets.
   154	    """
   155	
   156	    def __init__(self, *, clock=None, wall=None):
   157	        self.clock = clock or time.monotonic
   158	        self.wall = wall or time.time
   159	        self._lock = threading.RLock()
   160	        self._absolute_deadline = None
   161	        self._cleanup_deadline = None
   162	        self._inflight = []
   163	        self._inflight_lock = threading.Lock()
   164	        # The one way latches of the round.
   165	        self.cancel = threading.Event()
   166	        self.stopping = threading.Event()
   167	        self.unknown = threading.Event()
   168	
   169	    # -- the deadlines ----------------------------------------------------
   170	    def bind_absolute_deadline(self, deadline):
   171	        """Bind the absolute deadline of the round.  It may only shorten."""
   172	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
   173	        if value is None:
   174	            return self._absolute_deadline
   175	        with self._lock:
   176	            if self._absolute_deadline is None or value < self._absolute_deadline:
   177	                self._absolute_deadline = value
   178	            return self._absolute_deadline
   179	
   180	    @property
   181	    def absolute_deadline(self):
   182	        with self._lock:
   183	            return self._absolute_deadline
   184	
   185	    def cleanup_deadline_value(self):
   186	        """The published cleanup deadline, or ``None`` before the first."""
   187	        with self._lock:
   188	            return self._cleanup_deadline
   189	
   190	    def cleanup_deadline(self, deadline=None):
   191	        """Publish the ONE cleanup deadline of the round, atomically.
   192	
   193	        The deadline is the earlier of the absolute deadline of the
   194	        round, the fixed cleanup budget of the round and the deadline
   195	        that the caller brought.  It is generated **once**: every later
   196	        caller receives the very same value and may never extend it and
   197	        may never replace it.
   198	        """
   199	        with self._lock:
   200	            if self._cleanup_deadline is None:
   201	                value = self.clock() + CLEANUP_BUDGET_SECONDS
   202	                if self._absolute_deadline is not None:
   203	                    value = min(value, self._absolute_deadline)
   204	                candidate = _require_deadline(deadline)
   205	                if candidate is not None:
   206	                    value = min(value, candidate)
   207	                self._cleanup_deadline = value
   208	            return self._cleanup_deadline
   209	
   210	    def remaining_cleanup(self, deadline=None):
   211	        """The seconds that are left until the cleanup deadline.
   212	
   213	        The value is recomputed on every call: a wait that already
   214	        consumed part of the budget never receives the whole budget
   215	        again.
   216	        """
   217	        value = self.cleanup_deadline(deadline)
   218	        return max(0.0, value - self.clock())
   219	
   220	    def cleanup_expired(self):
   221	        """Whether the shared cleanup deadline of the round arrived."""
   222	        with self._lock:
   223	            value = self._cleanup_deadline
   224	        if value is None:
   225	            return False
   226	        return self.clock() >= value
   227	
   228	    def remaining_absolute(self):
   229	        """The seconds that are left until the absolute deadline.
   230	
   231	        The value is recomputed on every call and it is read from the
   232	        **one** context of the round: a layer that copied the number of
   233	        the deadline would have a deadline of its own.  ``None`` means
   234	        that the round did not bind an absolute deadline at all.
   235	        """
   236	        with self._lock:
   237	            value = self._absolute_deadline
   238	        if value is None:
   239	            return None
   240	        return max(0.0, value - self.clock())
   241	
   242	    def effective_deadline(self, call=None, bound=None):
   243	        """The ONE deadline that a call of a layer really uses.
   244	
   245	        The deadline of the call, the deadline that is bound to the
   246	        layer, the absolute deadline of the round and the **published**
   247	        cleanup deadline of the round are one value: the earliest of
   248	        the four.
   249	
   250	        The published cleanup deadline is part of it.  A round that
   251	        entered its cleanup publishes one deadline, and every layer that
   252	        reads the context afterwards is bound by it: a transport that
   253	        still waited with the long lease of the round would outlive the
   254	        only cleanup budget of the round.
   255	
   256	        The value is **recomputed on every call**: a deadline that was
   257	        published after the call started still shortens the call that is
   258	        running.
   259	        """
   260	        value = _require_deadline(call)
   261	        for candidate in (bound, self.absolute_deadline,
   262	                          self.cleanup_deadline_value()):
   263	            if candidate is None:
   264	                continue
   265	            if value is None or candidate < value:
   266	                value = candidate
   267	        return value
   268	
   269	    # -- the one way latches ----------------------------------------------
   270	    def request_cancel(self):
   271	        """Ask for the cancellation of the round.  One way."""
   272	        self.cancel.set()
   273	
   274	    def request_stop(self):
   275	        """Ask for the stop of the round.  One way."""
   276	        self.stopping.set()
   277	
   278	    def latch_unknown(self):
   279	        """Latch the unknown state of the round.  One way.
   280	
   281	        The first caller learns that it latched the state and every later
   282	        caller learns that the state was already latched.  A late event
   283	        may never clear the latch and may never replace it.
   284	        """
   285	        if self.unknown.is_set():
   286	            return False
   287	        self.unknown.set()
   288	        return True
   289	
   290	    def is_unknown(self):
   291	        """Whether the unknown state of the round was latched."""
   292	        return self.unknown.is_set()
   293	
   294	    # -- the owners of the operations in flight ---------------------------
   295	    def register_inflight(self, owner):
   296	        """Take the ownership of one operation that is in flight."""
   297	        with self._inflight_lock:
   298	            if owner not in self._inflight:
   299	                self._inflight.append(owner)
   300	        return owner
   301	
   302	    def release_inflight(self, owner):
   303	        """Give the ownership of one operation back."""
   304	        with self._inflight_lock:
   305	            if owner in self._inflight:
   306	                self._inflight.remove(owner)
   307	
   308	    def inflight(self):
   309	        """The owners of the operations that are in flight."""
   310	        with self._inflight_lock:
   311	            return list(self._inflight)
   312	
   313	
   314	# -- the Windows primitive table -------------------------------------------
   315	
   316	def _windows_command_line(executable, arguments):
   317	    """Build the command line of one child.
   318	
   319	    The quoting is the quoting of the Microsoft C runtime: a backslash run
   320	    in front of a quote is doubled and a trailing backslash run is doubled
   321	    as well, so that a path with a backslash is never truncated by the
   322	    parser of the child.
   323	    """
   324	    parts = [_quote_windows_argument(executable)]
   325	    parts.extend(_quote_windows_argument(item) for item in arguments)
   326	    return ' '.join(parts)
   327	
   328	
   329	def _quote_windows_argument(value):
   330	    text = str(value)
   331	    if text and not any(character in text for character in ' \t"'):
   332	        return text
   333	    quoted = ['"']
   334	    backslashes = 0
   335	    for character in text:
   336	        if character == '\\':
   337	            backslashes += 1
   338	            continue
   339	        if character == '"':
   340	            quoted.append('\\' * (backslashes * 2 + 1))
   341	            quoted.append('"')
   342	            backslashes = 0
   343	            continue
   344	        if backslashes:
   345	            quoted.append('\\' * backslashes)
   346	            backslashes = 0
   347	        quoted.append(character)
   348	    quoted.append('\\' * (backslashes * 2))
   349	    quoted.append('"')
   350	    return ''.join(quoted)
   351	
   352	
   353	def _environment_block(environment):
   354	    """Build the Unicode environment block of one child."""
   355	    if environment is None:
   356	        return None
   357	    import ctypes
   358	    items = sorted(environment.items(), key=lambda item: item[0].casefold())
   359	    raw = ''.join('%s=%s\0' % (key, value) for key, value in items) + '\0'
   360	    return ctypes.create_unicode_buffer(raw)
   361	
   362	
   363	def _windows_api_types():
   364	    """The pure declaration of the Windows structures of the broker.
   365	
   366	    The declaration is built **without** a native library.  It declares
   367	    the layout of the structures and it returns them together with the
   368	    ``ctypes`` module that built them, so that the layout can be measured
   369	    and checked off Windows and so that the loaded primitive table uses
   370	    exactly this declaration.
   371	
   372	    The fixed width types are declared **explicitly**.  ``ctypes.wintypes``
   373	    is a module of the host: its ``BOOL`` is a ``c_long`` and its
   374	    ``DWORD`` is a ``c_uint``, and the size of a ``c_long`` is not the
   375	    size of a Windows ``BOOL`` on every host.  A layout that was built
   376	    from the types of the host would therefore be a layout of the host
   377	    and not the layout of Windows.  Here a ``DWORD`` and a ``BOOL`` are
   378	    always 32 bit, a ``WORD`` is always 16 bit and a handle and a
   379	    ``SIZE_T`` have the width of a pointer.
   380	
   381	    A name of a class body is resolved in the class namespace and in the
   382	    global namespace only.  A class body therefore never assigns a name
   383	    that shadows an enclosing local: ``kernel32 = kernel32`` would raise
   384	    a ``NameError`` instead of copying the value, which is why the table
   385	    of the broker is built from an explicit namespace and never from the
   386	    body of a class.
   387	    """
   388	    import ctypes
   389	
   390	    DWORD = ctypes.c_uint32
   391	    BOOL = ctypes.c_int32
   392	    WORD = ctypes.c_uint16
   393	    UINT = ctypes.c_uint32
   394	    LONG = ctypes.c_int32
   395	    ULONG = ctypes.c_uint32
   396	    HANDLE = ctypes.c_void_p
   397	    SIZE_T = ctypes.c_size_t
   398	    ULONG_PTR = ctypes.c_size_t
   399	    LPCWSTR = ctypes.c_wchar_p
   400	    LPWSTR = ctypes.c_wchar_p
   401	
   402	    class STARTUPINFOW(ctypes.Structure):
   403	        _fields_ = [
   404	            ('cb', DWORD),
   405	            ('lpReserved', LPWSTR),
   406	            ('lpDesktop', LPWSTR),
   407	            ('lpTitle', LPWSTR),
   408	            ('dwX', DWORD),
   409	            ('dwY', DWORD),
   410	            ('dwXSize', DWORD),
   411	            ('dwYSize', DWORD),
   412	            ('dwXCountChars', DWORD),
   413	            ('dwYCountChars', DWORD),
   414	            ('dwFillAttribute', DWORD),
   415	            ('dwFlags', DWORD),
   416	            ('wShowWindow', WORD),
   417	            ('cbReserved2', WORD),
   418	            ('lpReserved2', ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_byte)),
   419	            ('hStdInput', HANDLE),
   420	            ('hStdOutput', HANDLE),
   421	            ('hStdError', HANDLE),
   422	        ]
   423	
   424	    class STARTUPINFOEXW(ctypes.Structure):
   425	        _fields_ = [('StartupInfo', STARTUPINFOW),
   426	                    ('lpAttributeList', ctypes.c_void_p)]
   427	
   428	    class PROCESS_INFORMATION(ctypes.Structure):
   429	        _fields_ = [('hProcess', HANDLE),
   430	                    ('hThread', HANDLE),
   431	                    ('dwProcessId', DWORD),
   432	                    ('dwThreadId', DWORD)]
   433	
   434	    class SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES(ctypes.Structure):
   435	        """The security attributes of one inheritable handle.
   436	
   437	        ``bInheritHandle`` is a ``BOOL`` and it is a 32 bit value: a
   438	        handle that is created with ``NULL`` security attributes can
   439	        never be inherited at all.
   440	        """
   441	        _fields_ = [('nLength', DWORD),
   442	                    ('lpSecurityDescriptor', ctypes.c_void_p),
   443	                    ('bInheritHandle', BOOL)]
   444	
   445	    class FILETIME(ctypes.Structure):
   446	        _fields_ = [('dwLowDateTime', DWORD),
   447	                    ('dwHighDateTime', DWORD)]
   448	
   449	    class OVERLAPPED(ctypes.Structure):
   450	        """The overlapped structure of one asynchronous operation.
   451	
   452	        The structure owns the event of the completion and the offset
   453	        of the operation.  It is released only after the completion of
   454	        the operation was proven.
   455	        """
   456	        _fields_ = [('Internal', SIZE_T),
   457	                    ('InternalHigh', SIZE_T),
   458	                    ('Offset', DWORD),
   459	                    ('OffsetHigh', DWORD),
   460	                    ('hEvent', HANDLE)]
   461	
   462	    class IO_COUNTERS(ctypes.Structure):
   463	        _fields_ = [('ReadOperationCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
   464	                    ('WriteOperationCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
   465	                    ('OtherOperationCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
   466	                    ('ReadTransferCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
   467	                    ('WriteTransferCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
   468	                    ('OtherTransferCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong)]
   469	
   470	    class JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION(ctypes.Structure):
   471	        _fields_ = [('PerProcessUserTimeLimit', ctypes.c_longlong),
   472	                    ('PerJobUserTimeLimit', ctypes.c_longlong),
   473	                    ('LimitFlags', DWORD),
   474	                    ('MinimumWorkingSetSize', SIZE_T),
   475	                    ('MaximumWorkingSetSize', SIZE_T),
   476	                    ('ActiveProcessLimit', DWORD),
   477	                    ('Affinity', SIZE_T),
   478	                    ('PriorityClass', DWORD),
   479	                    ('SchedulingClass', DWORD)]
   480	
   481	    class JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION(ctypes.Structure):
   482	        _fields_ = [('BasicLimitInformation', JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION),
   483	                    ('IoInfo', IO_COUNTERS),
   484	                    ('ProcessMemoryLimit', SIZE_T),
   485	                    ('JobMemoryLimit', SIZE_T),
   486	                    ('PeakProcessMemoryUsed', SIZE_T),
   487	                    ('PeakJobMemoryUsed', SIZE_T)]
   488	
   489	    class JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST(ctypes.Structure):
   490	        """The process id list of a job object.
   491	
   492	        The list is an **inline** array of ``ULONG_PTR`` and not a
   493	        pointer: the first process id is written at the offset of the
   494	        array and every other id follows it.  A structure that declared
   495	        a pointer field here would read the first id as an address and
   496	        would dereference it.
   497	        """
   498	        _fields_ = [('NumberOfAssignedProcesses', DWORD),
   499	                    ('NumberOfProcessIdsInList', DWORD),
   500	                    ('ProcessIdList', ULONG_PTR * 1)]
   501	
   502	    return {
   503	        'ctypes': ctypes,
   504	        'DWORD': DWORD,
   505	        'BOOL': BOOL,
   506	        'WORD': WORD,
   507	        'UINT': UINT,
   508	        'LONG': LONG,
   509	        'ULONG': ULONG,
   510	        'HANDLE': HANDLE,
   511	        'SIZE_T': SIZE_T,
   512	        'ULONG_PTR': ULONG_PTR,
   513	        'LPCWSTR': LPCWSTR,
   514	        'LPWSTR': LPWSTR,
   515	        'STARTUPINFOW': STARTUPINFOW,
   516	        'STARTUPINFOEXW': STARTUPINFOEXW,
   517	        'PROCESS_INFORMATION': PROCESS_INFORMATION,
   518	        'SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES': SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES,
   519	        'FILETIME': FILETIME,
   520	        'OVERLAPPED': OVERLAPPED,
   521	        'IO_COUNTERS': IO_COUNTERS,
   522	        'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION': JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION,
   523	        'JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION': JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION,
   524	        'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST': JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST,
   525	    }
   526	
   527	
   528	def _declare_windows_primitives(kernel32, types):
   529	    """Declare the prototypes of the Windows primitives of the broker.
   530	
   531	    Every prototype is explicit.  A handle is a ``HANDLE``, a pointer is
   532	    an explicit ``ctypes`` pointer, a size is a ``SIZE_T`` and every
   533	    value that Windows defines as a 32 bit number is a ``DWORD`` or a
   534	    ``BOOL``.  A primitive without a declared prototype takes its
   535	    arguments as ``c_int`` and would truncate a 64 bit handle.
   536	
   537	    The declaration is applied to the primitive table that is handed in,
   538	    so the very same code declares the table of a real run and the table
   539	    of an offline inspection.
   540	    """
   541	    ctypes = types['ctypes']
   542	    void_p = ctypes.c_void_p
   543	    size_t = types['SIZE_T']
   544	    handle = types['HANDLE']
   545	    boolean = types['BOOL']
   546	    dword = types['DWORD']
   547	    uint = types['UINT']
   548	    ulong = types['ULONG']
   549	
   550	    kernel32.CreateJobObjectW.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']),
   551	                                          types['LPCWSTR']]

   556	                                        ctypes.POINTER(types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']),
   557	                                        ctypes.POINTER(types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']),

   621	
   622	class _WindowsApiTable:
   623	    """The namespace of the primitive table of the broker.
   624	
   625	    The attributes are assigned explicitly.  A class body resolves a
   626	    name that it assigns itself in the class namespace and in the global
   627	    namespace only, so a class body that copies an enclosing local into
   628	    a name of its own raises a ``NameError`` instead of copying the
   629	    value.
   630	    """
   631	
   632	    def __init__(self, *, kernel32, types):
   633	        self.kernel32 = kernel32
   634	        self.types = dict(types)
   635	        for name in ('STARTUPINFOW', 'STARTUPINFOEXW', 'PROCESS_INFORMATION',
   636	                     'SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES', 'IO_COUNTERS',
   637	                     'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION',
   638	                     'JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION',
   639	                     'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST'):
   640	            setattr(self, name, types[name])
   641	
   642	    def __repr__(self):  # pragma: no cover - a diagnostic only
   643	        return '<WindowsApiTable types=%d>' % len(self.types)
   644	
   645	
   646	def _load_windows_api():
   647	    """Load the Windows primitive table of the broker.
   648	
   649	    The load is lazy and it happens only inside the explicit native entry.
   650	    An import of this module on a platform that is not Windows never
   651	    touches ``WinDLL`` and never resolves a symbol, so the module stays
   652	    importable and testable everywhere.  An unsupported platform refuses
   653	    here, before anything is created: it is never degraded into an
   654	    unconstrained process.
   655	
   656	    The declaration of the structures and the declaration of the
   657	    prototypes are two separate functions, so that the layout and the
   658	    prototypes can be inspected without loading a native library at all.
   659	    """
   660	    if os.name != 'nt':
   661	        raise BrokerError('BROKER_PLATFORM_UNSUPPORTED')
   662	    import ctypes
   663	    kernel32 = ctypes.WinDLL('kernel32', use_last_error=True)
   664	    types = _windows_api_types()
   665	    _declare_windows_primitives(kernel32, types)
   666	    return _WindowsApiTable(kernel32=kernel32, types=types)
   667	
   668	
   669	class CreatedProcess:
   670	    """The original handles of one created process."""
   671	
   672	    __slots__ = ('process', 'thread', 'pid', 'creation_time', 'failures')
   673	
   674	    def __init__(self, process, thread, pid, creation_time, failures=()):
   675	        self.process = process
   676	        self.thread = thread
   677	        self.pid = pid
   678	        self.creation_time = creation_time
   679	        self.failures = tuple(failures)
   680	
   681	
   682	class WindowsApi:
   683	    """The typed Windows primitive façade of the broker.
   684	
   685	    Every method of this class wraps exactly one Windows API call, and the
   686	    broker never calls a Windows function itself.  That keeps the native
   687	    detail - the pointer sizes, the handle ownership and the last error -
   688	    in one place, and it lets the offline validation drive the very same
   689	    broker logic through an in-process façade of the same shape.
   690	
   691	    The declarations are pointer size safe: a handle is a
   692	    ``wintypes.HANDLE``, every pointer is an explicit ``ctypes`` pointer
   693	    and every structure declares its fields.  The last error of a failed
   694	    call is read with ``ctypes.get_last_error()``, which is only correct
   695	    because the module was loaded with ``use_last_error=True``.
   696	    """
   697	
   698	    CREATE_SUSPENDED = 0x00000004
   699	    EXTENDED_STARTUPINFO_PRESENT = 0x00080000
   700	    STARTF_USESTDHANDLES = 0x00000100
   701	    CREATE_UNICODE_ENVIRONMENT = 0x00000400
   702	    JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE = 0x00002000
   703	    JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK = 0x01000000
   704	    JOB_OBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION = 9
   705	    JOB_OBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST = 3
   706	    WAIT_OBJECT_0 = 0
   707	    WAIT_TIMEOUT = 0x00000102
   708	    HANDLE_FLAG_INHERIT = 0x00000001
   709	    PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST = 0x00020002
   710	    GENERIC_READ = 0x80000000
   711	    FILE_SHARE_READ = 0x00000001
   712	    FILE_SHARE_WRITE = 0x00000002
   713	    OPEN_EXISTING = 3
   714	    FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL = 0x00000080
   715	    ERROR_MORE_DATA = 234
   716	    ERROR_BROKEN_PIPE = 109
   717	    ERROR_PIPE_NOT_CONNECTED = 233
   718	    ERROR_HANDLE_EOF = 38
   719	    ERROR_OPERATION_ABORTED = 995
   720	    ERROR_NOT_FOUND = 1168
   721	    DUPLICATE_SAME_ACCESS = 0x00000002
   722	
   723	    # -- the ownership states of one retained handle ----------------------
   724	    #: The façade still owns the handle and nobody else may take it.
   725	    API_OWNED = 'API_OWNED'
   726	    #: The recovery of the façade has **claimed** the handle and it is
   727	    #: preparing to close it.  A handover may not take it and a second
   728	    #: recovery may not claim it again while the claim is alive.
   729	    RECOVERY_CLAIMED = 'RECOVERY_CLAIMED'
   730	    #: The handover of the handle is running: the receiver registers it
   731	    #: and the façade forgets it in one atomic step.
   732	    TRANSFER_PENDING = 'TRANSFER_PENDING'
   733	    #: The handle was handed over to its new owner.  It is not in the
   734	    #: holding table any more and it is never closed by the recovery.
   735	    TRANSFERRED = 'TRANSFERRED'
   736	    #: The handle was closed with a confirmed close.
   737	    CLOSED = 'CLOSED'
   738	
   739	    def __init__(self, table=None):
   740	        """The typed façade of the Windows primitives.
   741	
   742	        The table is the primitive table of the broker.  A table may be
   743	        handed in for the inspection of the declaration and of the
   744	        layout: the table of a real run is the one that loads the
   745	        native library, and the table of an inspection never loads one.
   746	        The code that uses the façade is the same code in both cases.
   747	        """
   748	        self._api = table if table is not None else _load_windows_api()
   749	        # The invalid value of a handle has the width of a pointer: it is
   750	        # ``-1`` as a 32 bit value and it is ``0xffffffffffffffff`` as a
   751	        # 64 bit one.  A comparison with the Python value ``-1`` would
   752	        # never match the 64 bit value.
   753	        self.invalid_handle_value = self._api.types['ctypes'].c_void_p(-1).value
   754	        # The holding table of the façade.  Every original handle that a
   755	        # successful ``CreateProcess`` produced enters this table in the
   756	        # moment of its creation, before anything else may fail.  A handle
   757	        # that was handed over to its owner leaves the table; a handle
   758	        # that could not be handed over stays in it, with its owner, its
   759	        # pid and the failures of its release.  The table is the recovery
   760	        # entry point of the façade: the core keeps the façade and it can
   761	        # therefore always reach the handles that it never received.
   762	        self._retained = {}
   763	        self._retained_order = []
   764	        # The one lock of the holding table.  Every mutation of the
   765	        # table and every handover of a handle out of it happen under
   766	        # this lock: a handle that is registered by its receiver and
   767	        # that the façade still names would have **two** owners, and a
   768	        # handle that left the façade before its receiver registered it
   769	        # would have none.
   770	        self._table_lock = threading.RLock()
   771	        # The handovers that really happened.  The list is the evidence
   772	        # of the unique owner of a handle: a handle that was handed
   773	        # over is not in the holding table any more and it is never
   774	        # closed by the recovery of the façade.
   775	        self._handovers = []
   776	        # The ONE deadline of the recovery of the façade.  It is
   777	        # published once: a second recovery of the same round reuses
   778	        # the very same value and it never opens a new budget.  It may
   779	        # only be **shortened** by a context that arrived later.
   780	        self._recovery_deadline = None
   781	        # The absolute deadline of the round, when the façade is bound to
   782	        # one.  Every wait of the façade is bounded by it: a wait that
   783	        # does not fit into the deadline of the round never starts.
   784	        self._deadline = None
   785	        # The ONE context of the round.  The façade reads the absolute
   786	        # deadline and the published cleanup deadline of the round from
   787	        # it and it never keeps a budget of its own.
   788	        self._context = None
   789	        self._clock = time.monotonic
   790	
   791	    def bind_deadline(self, deadline, clock=None):
   792	        """Bind the absolute deadline of the round to this façade.
   793	
   794	        The deadline may only shorten the deadline that is already
   795	        bound.  The waits of the façade - the wait of a release and the
   796	        wait of the recovery - are bounded by it and they never start a
   797	        wait with a fresh timeout of their own.
   798	        """
   799	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
   800	        if value is None:
   801	            return self._deadline
   802	        if clock is not None:
   803	            self._clock = clock
   804	        if self._deadline is None or value < self._deadline:
   805	            self._deadline = value
   806	        return self._deadline
   807	
   808	    def bind_context(self, context):
   809	        """Bind the ONE context of the round to this façade.
   810	
   811	        The façade reads the absolute deadline and the published
   812	        cleanup deadline of the round from the context and it never
   813	        keeps a budget of its own: a recovery that opened a fresh
   814	        budget of its own would outlive the round that it recovers.
   815	        """
   816	        if context is not None:
   817	            self._context = context
   818	        return self._context
   819	
   820	    def _effective_deadline(self, call=None):
   821	        """The ONE deadline that a wait of this façade really uses.
   822	
   823	        The deadline of the call, the deadline that is bound to the
   824	        façade, the absolute deadline of the round and the **published**
   825	        cleanup deadline of the round are one value: the earliest of
   826	        the four.  The value is recomputed on every call, so a deadline
   827	        that was published after the wait started still shortens it.
   828	        """
   829	        value = _require_deadline(call)
   830	        bound = self._deadline
   831	        if bound is not None and (value is None or bound < value):
   832	            value = bound
   833	        context = self._context
   834	        if context is not None:
   835	            absolute = context.absolute_deadline
   836	            if absolute is not None and (value is None or absolute < value):
   837	                value = absolute
   838	            cleanup = context.cleanup_deadline_value()
   839	            if cleanup is not None and (value is None or cleanup < value):
   840	                value = cleanup
   841	        return value
   842	
   843	    def _wait_timeout_ms(self):
   844	        """The milliseconds that are left until the bound deadline.
   845	
   846	        A façade without a bound deadline keeps the closed wait of the
   847	        package.  A façade with one never waits longer than the deadline
   848	        of the round allows.
   849	
   850	        The milliseconds are **floored** and they are never rounded
   851	        **up**: a remainder that is smaller than the representable unit
   852	        is zero and it asks for no wait at all.  A wait of one
   853	        millisecond with a remainder of a fraction of a millisecond
   854	        would outlive the round that it belongs to.
   855	        """
   856	        value = self._effective_deadline()
   857	        if value is None:
   858	            return WAIT_MILLISECONDS
   859	        remaining = value - self._clock()
   860	        if remaining <= 0:
   861	            return 0
   862	        return min(WAIT_MILLISECONDS, int(remaining * 1000))
   863	
   864	    def _last_error(self):
   865	        """The last error of the last failing primitive of the host.
   866	
   867	        The value is read with ``ctypes.get_last_error()``, which is
   868	        only correct because the module was loaded with
   869	        ``use_last_error=True``.  A host that does not have that
   870	        function at all cannot report a last error and reports zero.
   871	        """
   872	        getter = getattr(self._api.types['ctypes'], 'get_last_error', None)
   873	        if getter is None:
   874	            return 0
   875	        return int(getter())
   876	
   877	    def _security_attributes(self, inherit):
   878	        """The security attributes of one handle.
   879	
   880	        The attributes say that the handle may be inherited.  A handle
   881	        that is created with ``NULL`` security attributes is never
   882	        inheritable, so the handle list of the process attributes would
   883	        name a handle that the child cannot receive.
   884	        """
   885	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
   886	        attributes = self._api.types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']()
   887	        attributes.nLength = ctypes.sizeof(self._api.types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES'])
   888	        attributes.lpSecurityDescriptor = None
   889	        attributes.bInheritHandle = bool(inherit)
   890	        return attributes
   891	
   892	    # -- the holding table of the original handles -------------------------
   893	    def _retain(self, handle, kind, pid=None, stage='CREATED'):
   894	        """Take one original handle into the holding table of the façade.
   895	
   896	        The table is entered in the moment of the creation of the handle
   897	        and it is left only when the handle was closed with a confirmed
   898	        close or when it was handed over to its owner.  A handle that
   899	        stays in the table is a resource that this round still owns and
   900	        it is named with its kind, its pid and its failures.
   901	        """
   902	        key = id(handle)
   903	        with self._table_lock:
   904	            if key not in self._retained:
   905	                self._retained_order.append(key)
   906	            self._retained[key] = {'handle': handle, 'kind': kind, 'pid': pid,
   907	                                   'stage': stage, 'owner': 'WINDOWS_API',
   908	                                   'state': self.API_OWNED,
   909	                                   'failures': []}
   910	            return self._retained[key]
   911	
   912	    def _forget(self, handle):
   913	        """Leave the holding table: the handle is closed or handed over."""
   914	        key = id(handle)
   915	        with self._table_lock:
   916	            if key in self._retained:
   917	                del self._retained[key]
   918	                if key in self._retained_order:
   919	                    self._retained_order.remove(key)
   920	                return True
   921	        return False
   922	
   923	    def forget_handle(self, handle):
   924	        """Leave the holding table, under the lock of the table.
   925	
   926	        The method is the public face of ``_forget``: the receiver of a
   927	        handle uses it when the façade of the primitives does not offer
   928	        the atomic handover.  A façade that offers the handover uses
   929	        that one instead.
   930	        """
   931	        return self._forget(handle)
   932	
   933	    def _note(self, handle, failure):
   934	        """Name one failure of one retained handle."""
   935	        key = id(handle)
   936	        with self._table_lock:
   937	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
   938	            if entry is not None:
   939	                entry['failures'].append(failure)
   940	            return entry
   941	
   942	    def retained_handles(self):
   943	        """The original handles that this façade still holds.
   944	
   945	        The list is a copy: a caller that changes it changes nothing of
   946	        the holding table of the façade.
   947	        """
   948	        with self._table_lock:
   949	            return [dict(self._retained[key]) for key in self._retained_order
   950	                    if key in self._retained]
   951	
   952	    def handovers(self):
   953	        """The handovers that really happened, as a copy of the log."""
   954	        with self._table_lock:
   955	            return [dict(item) for item in self._handovers]
   956	
   957	    def handover(self, entries, *, owner, register):
   958	        """Hand the named handles over to their new owner, in one step.
   959	
   960	        The receiver registers the handles and the façade forgets them
   961	        under **one** lock.  The two facts are one atomic step: a
   962	        handle that the receiver registered and that the façade still
   963	        names would have two independent owners, and the recovery of
   964	        the façade could close a handle that a live reader of the round
   965	        is reading from.  A handle that left the façade before its
   966	        receiver registered it would have no owner at all.
   967	
   968	        The handover and the recovery of the façade are **the same
   969	        ownership decision**.  A handle that the recovery has already
   970	        claimed is refused here: the recovery is preparing to close it
   971	        and a second owner may never appear.  A refused handle stays
   972	        with the façade and it is never registered with the receiver.
   973	
   974	        A registration that fails leaves the handle with the façade:
   975	        the state of the entry is restored and the handle is never
   976	        half registered and closed by two owners at the same time.
   977	
   978	        ``entries`` is the list of ``(handle, kind)`` pairs and
   979	        ``register`` receives it and registers every handle with the
   980	        receiver.  The handover is recorded, so that the evidence of the
   981	        round can name the owner that received the handle.
   982	        """
   983	        items = list(entries)
   984	        refused = []
   985	        with self._table_lock:
   986	            accepted = []
   987	            for handle, kind in items:
   988	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
   989	                if entry is not None and entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED:
   990	                    # The recovery of the façade owns this handle and it
   991	                    # is preparing to close it: the handover may not
   992	                    # take it and the receiver may never name it.
   993	                    refused.append((handle, kind))
   994	                    continue
   995	                if entry is not None:
   996	                    entry['state'] = self.TRANSFER_PENDING
   997	                accepted.append((handle, kind))
   998	            try:
   999	                register(accepted)
  1000	            except BaseException:
  1001	                # The receiver did not register the handles: they stay
  1002	                # with the façade and their state is restored.  A half
  1003	                # registered handle is never closed by two owners.
  1004	                for handle, _kind in accepted:
  1005	                    entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
  1006	                    if entry is not None:
  1007	                        entry['state'] = self.API_OWNED
  1008	                raise
  1009	            for handle, kind in accepted:
  1010	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
  1011	                if entry is not None:
  1012	                    entry['state'] = self.TRANSFERRED
  1013	                self._forget(handle)
  1014	                self._handovers.append({'kind': kind, 'owner': owner,
  1015	                                        'handle_repr': repr(handle)})
  1016	        if refused:
  1017	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDOVER_CLAIMED',
  1018	                              ','.join(repr(handle) for handle, _kind in refused))
  1019	        return True
  1020	
  1021	    def _claim_entry(self, key):
  1022	        """Claim one retained entry for the recovery of the façade.
  1023	
  1024	        The claim is **one atomic step** under the lock of the table:
  1025	        an entry that another recovery already claimed is never claimed
  1026	        a second time, and an entry that a handover took is never
  1027	        claimed at all.  The claim does not hold the lock of the table
  1028	        while it waits: the blocking primitive of the recovery runs
  1029	        outside of the lock and the table stays usable.
  1030	        """
  1031	        with self._table_lock:
  1032	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
  1033	            if entry is None:
  1034	                return None
  1035	            if entry.get('state') != self.API_OWNED:
  1036	                # The entry is claimed by a recovery that is running or
  1037	                # it is in the middle of a handover: it is not the owner
  1038	                # of this recovery.
  1039	                return None
  1040	            entry['state'] = self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED
  1041	            return dict(entry)
  1042	
  1043	    def _decide_entries(self, entries, state):
  1044	        """One table decision for a set of retained entries.
  1045	
  1046	        The decision is **one atomic step** under the lock of the table
  1047	        and it covers **every** named entry at once: the process and
  1048	        the thread of one creation are decided together and a decision
  1049	        that reserved one of them and refused the other one is never
  1050	        taken.
  1051	
  1052	        The entries that the decision reserved leave the table in the
  1053	        state ``state`` and the entries that it refused keep their own
  1054	        state.  An entry that is **not** in the table any more is
  1055	        refused as well: it was already closed or already handed over
  1056	        and it may never be delivered or closed a second time.
  1057	
  1058	        The lock of the table is **never** held while a blocking
  1059	        primitive runs: the decision is taken, the primitive runs
  1060	        outside of the lock and the table stays usable.
  1061	        """
  1062	        reserved = []
  1063	        refused = []
  1064	        with self._table_lock:
  1065	            for handle, kind in entries:
  1066	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
  1067	                if entry is None:
  1068	                    refused.append((handle, kind, 'GONE'))
  1069	                    continue
  1070	                current = entry.get('state')
  1071	                if current == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED:
  1072	                    # The recovery of the façade owns this handle and it
  1073	                    # is preparing to close it: nobody else may take it.
  1074	                    refused.append((handle, kind, 'RECOVERY_CLAIMED'))
  1075	                    continue
  1076	                if current != self.API_OWNED:
  1077	                    refused.append((handle, kind, str(current)))
  1078	                    continue
  1079	                entry['state'] = state
  1080	                reserved.append((handle, kind))
  1081	        return reserved, refused
  1082	
  1083	    def _restore_entries(self, entries, state):
  1084	        """Give the reserved entries back to the table of the façade.
  1085	
  1086	        A decision that could not be completed gives its entries back:
  1087	        the entries keep their original reference and a later recovery
  1088	        of the round may claim them again.
  1089	        """
  1090	        with self._table_lock:
  1091	            for handle, _kind in entries:
  1092	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
  1093	                if entry is None:
  1094	                    continue
  1095	                if entry.get('state') == self.TRANSFER_PENDING:
  1096	                    entry['state'] = state
  1097	        return True
  1098	
  1099	    def _commit_entries(self, entries):
  1100	        """The reserved entries left the table: they are the property of
  1101	        their new owner.
  1102	
  1103	        The handover of the entries is recorded and it happens under the
  1104	        lock of the table: a handle that the receiver registered and
  1105	        that the façade still names would have two owners.
  1106	        """
  1107	        with self._table_lock:
  1108	            for handle, kind in entries:
  1109	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
  1110	                if entry is not None:
  1111	                    entry['state'] = self.TRANSFERRED
  1112	                self._forget(handle)
  1113	                self._handovers.append({'kind': kind, 'owner': 'WINDOWS_API',
  1114	                                        'handle_repr': repr(handle)})
  1115	        return True
  1116	
  1117	    def _release_claim(self, key):
  1118	        """Give a claim back: a later recovery may claim the entry again."""
  1119	        with self._table_lock:
  1120	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
  1121	            if entry is not None and entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED:
  1122	                entry['state'] = self.API_OWNED
  1123	
  1124	    def _owns_claim(self, key):
  1125	        """Whether the claim of this recovery is still the owner of the entry."""
  1126	        with self._table_lock:
  1127	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
  1128	            if entry is None:
  1129	                return False
  1130	            return entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED
  1131	
  1132	    def _recovery_timeout_ms(self):
  1133	        """The milliseconds that are left until the ONE recovery deadline.
  1134	
  1135	        The deadline of the recovery is published **once**: a second
  1136	        recovery of the same round reuses the very same value and it
  1137	        never opens a new budget of its own.  It may only be **shortened**
  1138	        by a context that arrived later: a recovery that re-read the
  1139	        context of the round may not use the long lease of the round
  1140	        after the round published its cleanup deadline.
  1141	
  1142	        A wait of a recovery that arrives after the deadline never
  1143	        starts.
  1144	        """
  1145	        with self._table_lock:
  1146	            current = self._effective_deadline()
  1147	            if self._recovery_deadline is None:
  1148	                value = current
  1149	                if value is None:
  1150	                    value = self._clock() + (WAIT_MILLISECONDS / 1000.0)
  1151	                self._recovery_deadline = min(
  1152	                    value, self._clock() + (WAIT_MILLISECONDS / 1000.0))
  1153	            elif current is not None and current < self._recovery_deadline:
  1154	                self._recovery_deadline = current
  1155	            remaining = self._recovery_deadline - self._clock()
  1156	        if remaining <= 0:
  1157	            return 0
  1158	        # The milliseconds are floored and they are never rounded up: a
  1159	        # wait of one millisecond with a remainder of a fraction of a
  1160	        # millisecond outlives the round that it belongs to.
  1161	        return min(WAIT_MILLISECONDS, int(remaining * 1000))
  1162	
  1163	    def _confirm_process_exit(self, handle, failures, cancellations,
  1164	                              timeout_ms=None):
  1165	        """Try to confirm the exit of one process, once.
  1166	
  1167	        The confirmation of the exit and the confirmation of the close
  1168	        of a handle are two different facts.  A wait that timed out is
  1169	        **not** an exit: the process may still be alive and it may even
  1170	        be a suspended process that never ran.  Its control handle is
  1171	        the only handle that may stop it.
  1172	
  1173	        The method returns ``True`` only when the exit was really
  1174	        confirmed.  A cancellation of the wait is named and it is handed
  1175	        to the caller, which raises it after every safe step was
  1176	        executed.
  1177	        """
  1178	        if timeout_ms is None:
  1179	            timeout_ms = self._wait_timeout_ms()
  1180	        if timeout_ms <= 0:
  1181	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:no_budget')
  1182	            return False
  1183	        try:
  1184	            result = self._api.kernel32.WaitForSingleObject(handle, timeout_ms)
  1185	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
  1186	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:CANCELLED')
  1187	            cancellations.append(error)
  1188	            return False
  1189	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  1190	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:' + type(error).__name__)
  1191	            return False
  1192	        if result == self.WAIT_OBJECT_0:
  1193	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:exited')
  1194	            return True
  1195	        if result == self.WAIT_TIMEOUT:
  1196	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:exit_not_confirmed')
  1197	            return False
  1198	        failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:wait=%d' % int(result))
  1199	        return False
  1200	
  1201	    def release_retained(self):
  1202	        """Try to release every handle that the façade still holds.
  1203	
  1204	        This is the recovery entry point of the façade.  It is explicit:
  1205	        a caller that wants the handles of a failed creation released
  1206	        asks for it, and the result is named.  A handle that could not
  1207	        be closed stays in the holding table.
  1208	
  1209	        Every handle is released on its own and every step of one handle
  1210	        is independent of the others: a cancellation in the middle of the
  1211	        recovery never skips the close of the next handle.  The
  1212	        cancellation is raised at the end of the recovery, after every
  1213	        safe step was executed, and it never replaces an ordinary
  1214	        failure of a handle.
  1215	
  1216	        The **control handle of a process** is released only when its
  1217	        exit was really confirmed.  A process whose exit was not
  1218	        confirmed may still be alive and its control handle is the only
  1219	        handle that may stop it.  The handle therefore stays in the
  1220	        holding table with its owner and the recovery of the façade can
  1221	        reach it again: a later recovery that confirms the exit closes
  1222	        it and forgets it.  A close that succeeds is never a proof that
  1223	        the process ended.
  1224	
  1225	        The recovery and the handover are **one ownership decision**.
  1226	        Every entry is **claimed** under the lock of the table before
  1227	        the recovery touches it, and a claim that is alive keeps every
  1228	        other recovery and every handover away from the entry.  The
  1229	        blocking primitive of the recovery runs **outside** of the lock
  1230	        of the table: the table is never held while the recovery waits.
  1231	        The claim is verified again before the close: an entry that
  1232	        changed its owner in the meantime is never closed.
  1233	        """
  1234	        report = []
  1235	        cancellations = []
  1236	        for key in list(self._retained_order):
  1237	            claimed = self._claim_entry(key)
  1238	            if claimed is None:
  1239	                # The entry is not owned by this recovery: it is claimed
  1240	                # by a recovery that is running or it was handed over.
  1241	                # It is never closed here.
  1242	                entry = self._retained.get(key)
  1243	                if entry is None:
  1244	                    continue
  1245	                report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
  1246	                               'released': False,
  1247	                               'failures': ['entry_not_claimed:' + str(
  1248	                                   entry.get('state'))]})
  1249	                continue
  1250	            entry = claimed
  1251	            handle = entry['handle']
  1252	            failures = []
  1253	            try:
  1254	                if entry['kind'] == 'process':
  1255	                    try:
  1256	                        if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateProcess(handle, 1):
  1257	                            failures.append('TerminateProcess:last_error=%d'
  1258	                                            % self._last_error())
  1259	                    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
  1260	                        failures.append('TerminateProcess:CANCELLED')
  1261	                        cancellations.append(error)
  1262	                    except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  1263	                        failures.append('TerminateProcess:' + type(error).__name__)
  1264	                    confirmed = self._confirm_process_exit(
  1265	                        handle, failures, cancellations,
  1266	                        timeout_ms=self._recovery_timeout_ms())
  1267	                    if not confirmed:
  1268	                        # The process may still be alive: the control
  1269	                        # handle stays with the façade and it is never
  1270	                        # closed under a process that nobody may stop
  1271	                        # any more.
  1272	                        failures.append('process_control_handle_retained:'
  1273	                                        'exit_not_confirmed')
  1274	                        self._note(handle, '|'.join(failures))
  1275	                        report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
  1276	                                       'released': False,
  1277	                                       'failures': list(failures)})
  1278	                        continue
  1279	                # The claim is verified again before the close: an entry
  1280	                # that changed its owner while the recovery waited is
  1281	                # never closed by this recovery.
  1282	                if not self._owns_claim(key):
  1283	                    failures.append('entry_claim_lost:before_close')
  1284	                    report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
  1285	                                   'released': False,
  1286	                                   'failures': list(failures)})
  1287	                    continue
  1288	                if self._close_quietly(handle, failures, cancellations):
  1289	                    self._forget(handle)
  1290	                else:
  1291	                    self._note(handle, '|'.join(failures))
  1292	                report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
  1293	                               'released': not any(failure.startswith('CloseHandle')
  1294	                                                   for failure in failures),
  1295	                               'failures': list(failures)})
  1296	            finally:
  1297	                # A claim that did not end with a confirmed close is
  1298	                # given back: a later recovery of the round may claim
  1299	                # the entry again and it may confirm the exit.
  1300	                self._release_claim(key)
  1301	        if cancellations:
  1302	            # The cancellation is raised after every safe step of the
  1303	            # recovery was executed.  It is never swallowed and it never
  1304	            # replaces an ordinary failure that is named in the report.
  1305	            raise cancellations[0]
  1306	        return report
  1307	
  1308	    # -- the job object ---------------------------------------------------
  1309	    def create_job(self):
  1310	        handle = self._api.kernel32.CreateJobObjectW(None, None)
  1311	        if not handle:
  1312	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_CREATE_FAILED',
  1313	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
  1314	        return handle
  1315	
  1316	    def set_job_kill_on_close(self, job):
  1317	        """Set the kill-on-close limit of the job and nothing else.
  1318	
  1319	        ``JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK`` is deliberately *not* set: a
  1320	        child of the round may not break away from the job.
  1321	        """
  1322	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
  1323	        limit = self._api.JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION()
  1324	        limit.BasicLimitInformation.LimitFlags = self.JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE
  1325	        if not self._api.kernel32.SetInformationJobObject(
  1326	                job, self.JOB_OBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION,
  1327	                ctypes.byref(limit), ctypes.sizeof(limit)):
  1328	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_LIMIT_FAILED',
  1329	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
  1330	        return True
  1331	
  1332	    def terminate_job(self, job, exit_code):
  1333	        if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateJobObject(job, exit_code):
  1334	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_TERMINATE_FAILED',
  1335	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
  1336	        return True
  1337	
  1338	    def job_process_ids(self, job):
  1339	        """The bounded list of the process ids of the job.
  1340	
  1341	        The query is bounded by the descendant cap of this package.  A
  1342	        job that holds more processes than the cap is *not* reported as an
  1343	        empty job: the answer is unknown, and an unknown answer never
  1344	        becomes a zero.
  1345	
  1346	        The structure of Windows holds two counts and an **inline** array
  1347	        of ``ULONG_PTR``.  The array is read from the buffer at the
  1348	        offset of the array and never through a pointer field.
  1349	
  1350	        The answer is only accepted when **every** fact of it agrees:
  1351	
  1352	        * the returned length is at least the size of the header, so that
  1353	          the two counts were really written and a legal empty job still
  1354	          carries its complete header;
  1355	        * the returned length is at most the size of the buffer, so that
  1356	          a length that is larger than the buffer was never accepted;
  1357	        * the returned length is at least the length that the count of
  1358	          the list needs, so that the list was not truncated;
  1359	        * the two counts are equal, so that the number of the assigned
  1360	          processes and the number of the listed processes agree;
  1361	        * the count of the list is within the cap of this package.
  1362	
  1363	        Every other result is **unknown**: an exception, a returned
  1364	        length of zero, a reversed pair of counts, a count over the cap
  1365	        and a truncated list.  An unknown answer is never an empty job
  1366	        and never a success.
  1367	        """
  1368	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
  1369	        capacity = MAX_DESCENDANTS + 1
  1370	        pointer_size = ctypes.sizeof(ctypes.c_size_t)
  1371	        header_size = 2 * ctypes.sizeof(self._api.types['DWORD'])
  1372	        size = header_size + capacity * pointer_size
  1373	        buffer = ctypes.create_string_buffer(size)
  1374	        returned = self._api.types['DWORD'](0)
  1375	        if not self._api.kernel32.QueryInformationJobObject(
  1376	                job, self.JOB_OBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST, buffer, size,
  1377	                ctypes.byref(returned)):
  1378	            error = self._last_error()
  1379	            if error == self.ERROR_MORE_DATA:
  1380	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT', 'last_error=%d' % error)
  1381	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_UNKNOWN', 'last_error=%d' % error)
  1382	        header = ctypes.cast(buffer, ctypes.POINTER(
  1383	            self._api.JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST)).contents
  1384	        assigned = int(header.NumberOfAssignedProcesses)
  1385	        listed = int(header.NumberOfProcessIdsInList)
  1386	        reported = int(returned.value)
  1387	        # The returned length has to carry the whole header and it may
  1388	        # never be larger than the buffer that was handed to Windows.
  1389	        if reported < header_size or reported > size:
  1390	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
  1391	                              'returned=%d:header=%d:allocated=%d'
  1392	                              % (reported, header_size, size))
  1393	        if assigned > capacity or listed > capacity:
  1394	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT',
  1395	                              'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
  1396	        if assigned != listed:
  1397	            # The two counts disagree: the answer is unknown and it is
  1398	            # never an empty job.
  1399	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
  1400	                              'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
  1401	        if listed > MAX_DESCENDANTS:
  1402	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT',
  1403	                              'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
  1404	        needed = header_size + listed * pointer_size
  1405	        if needed > reported:
  1406	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
  1407	                              'returned=%d:needed=%d' % (reported, needed))
  1408	        if listed == 0:
  1409	            # A legal empty job: the header was complete and the two
  1410	            # counts were zero.
  1411	            return []
  1412	        offset = header_size // pointer_size
  1413	        identifiers = ctypes.cast(buffer, ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_size_t))
  1414	        return [int(identifiers[offset + index]) for index in range(listed)]
  1415	
  1416	    # -- the processes -----------------------------------------------------
  1417	    def create_process_suspended(self, *, executable, arguments, working_directory,
  1418	                                  stdout_handle, stderr_handle, environment=None):
  1419	        """Create one suspended process and return its original handles.
  1420	
  1421	        The child inherits exactly three handles: the write end of its
  1422	        standard output, the write end of its standard error and the null
  1423	        device as its standard input.  The inheritance set is passed
  1424	        explicitly as the handle list of the process attributes, so that
  1425	        the control channel of the broker, the job handle and every other
  1426	        handle of the broker stay in the broker.
  1427	
  1428	        The size of the start up information is the size of the whole
  1429	        ``STARTUPINFOEXW``, because the extended flag is set and the
  1430	        attribute list follows the start up information inside it.
  1431	
  1432	        The creation has **independent** steps.  The deletion of the
  1433	        attribute list, the close of the null device, the birth query of
  1434	        the created process and the release of a process that could not
  1435	        be adopted do not depend on each other: a failure of one of them
  1436	        never skips the others.  The first error of the creation is the
  1437	        error that leaves the method and the later errors are named next
  1438	        to it; the first error is never replaced by a later one.
  1439	
  1440	        The original handles of a created process enter the holding table
  1441	        of this façade in the moment of the creation.  The null device
  1442	        of the child enters it in the moment of its creation as well: a
  1443	        null device that could not be closed is a resource of this round
  1444	        and it stays with its owner.  A handle that was handed over to
  1445	        the caller leaves the table; a handle that could not be closed
  1446	        stays in it with its owner and its failures, so that the recovery
  1447	        of the façade can always reach it.
  1448	        """
  1449	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
  1450	        kernel32 = self._api.kernel32
  1451	        failures = []
  1452	        command = ctypes.create_unicode_buffer(_windows_command_line(executable, arguments))
  1453	        environment_block = _environment_block(environment)
  1454	        attributes = self._security_attributes(True)
  1455	        null = kernel32.CreateFileW('NUL', self.GENERIC_READ,
  1456	                                    self.FILE_SHARE_READ | self.FILE_SHARE_WRITE,
  1457	                                    ctypes.byref(attributes), self.OPEN_EXISTING,
  1458	                                    self.FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL, None)
  1459	        if not null or null == self.invalid_handle_value:
  1460	            error = self._last_error()
  1461	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_NULL_DEVICE_FAILED',
  1462	                              'last_error=%d' % error)
  1463	        # The moment of the creation of the null device.  The handle of
  1464	        # the null device enters the holding table of this façade right
  1465	        # here: a null device whose close fails later is a resource of
  1466	        # this round and it keeps its original owner and its recovery
  1467	        # entry point.
  1468	        self._retain(null, 'null_device')
  1469	        attribute_list = None
  1470	        information = None
  1471	        process = None
  1472	        thread = None
  1473	        pid = None
  1474	        primary = None
  1475	        cleanup_error = None
  1476	        cancellations = []
  1477	        try:
  1478	            try:
  1479	                inherited = (ctypes.c_void_p * 3)(stdout_handle, stderr_handle, null)
  1480	                size = ctypes.c_size_t(0)
  1481	                kernel32.InitializeProcThreadAttributeList(None, 1, 0, ctypes.byref(size))
  1482	                attribute_list = ctypes.create_string_buffer(size.value)
  1483	                if not kernel32.InitializeProcThreadAttributeList(
  1484	                        attribute_list, 1, 0, ctypes.byref(size)):
  1485	                    error = self._last_error()
  1486	                    raise BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_FAILED',
  1487	                                      'last_error=%d' % error)
  1488	                if not kernel32.UpdateProcThreadAttribute(
  1489	                        attribute_list, 0, self.PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST,
  1490	                        inherited, ctypes.sizeof(inherited), None, None):
  1491	                    error = self._last_error()
  1492	                    raise BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_FAILED',
  1493	                                      'last_error=%d' % error)
  1494	                startup = self._api.STARTUPINFOEXW()
  1495	                # The extended start up information is passed, so the
  1496	                # size is the size of the whole structure and not the
  1497	                # size of the plain start up information.
  1498	                startup.StartupInfo.cb = ctypes.sizeof(self._api.STARTUPINFOEXW)
  1499	                startup.StartupInfo.dwFlags = self.STARTF_USESTDHANDLES
  1500	                startup.StartupInfo.hStdInput = null
  1501	                startup.StartupInfo.hStdOutput = stdout_handle
  1502	                startup.StartupInfo.hStdError = stderr_handle
  1503	                startup.lpAttributeList = ctypes.cast(attribute_list, ctypes.c_void_p)
  1504	                information = self._api.PROCESS_INFORMATION()
  1505	                flags = (self.CREATE_SUSPENDED | self.EXTENDED_STARTUPINFO_PRESENT
  1506	                         | self.CREATE_UNICODE_ENVIRONMENT)
  1507	                environment_pointer = (ctypes.cast(environment_block, ctypes.c_void_p)
  1508	                                       if environment_block is not None else None)
  1509	                created = kernel32.CreateProcessW(
  1510	                    str(executable), command, None, None, True, flags,
  1511	                    environment_pointer, str(working_directory),
  1512	                    ctypes.byref(startup.StartupInfo), ctypes.byref(information))
  1513	                if not created:
  1514	                    error = self._last_error()
  1515	                    raise BrokerError('BROKER_PROCESS_CREATE_FAILED',
  1516	                                      'last_error=%d' % error)
  1517	                # The moment of the creation.  The original handles of
  1518	                # the process enter the holding table of this façade
  1519	                # before anything else may fail: they are never only an
  1520	                # integer in a local list or in the detail of an error.
  1521	                process = information.hProcess
  1522	                thread = information.hThread
  1523	                pid = int(information.dwProcessId)
  1524	                self._retain(process, 'process', pid)
  1525	                self._retain(thread, 'thread', pid)
  1526	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  1527	                primary = error
  1528	            finally:
  1529	                # The cleanups of the creation are independent of each
  1530	                # other and independent of the failure that is on the way
  1531	                # out.  A cleanup that fails never skips the other one and
  1532	                # a cancellation in the middle of a cleanup never skips
  1533	                # the cleanup that follows it: the cancellation is named
  1534	                # and it is raised after every safe step was executed.
  1535	                attribute_failures = []
  1536	                if attribute_list is not None:
  1537	                    try:
  1538	                        kernel32.DeleteProcThreadAttributeList(attribute_list)
  1539	                    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
  1540	                        attribute_failures.append('DeleteProcThreadAttributeList:CANCELLED')
  1541	                        cancellations.append(error)
  1542	                    except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  1543	                        attribute_failures.append('DeleteProcThreadAttributeList:'
  1544	                                                  + type(error).__name__)
  1545	                null_failures = []
  1546	                if self._close_quietly(null, null_failures, cancellations):
  1547	                    # The null device was closed with a confirmed close:
  1548	                    # it may be forgotten.
  1549	                    self._forget(null)
  1550	                failures.extend(attribute_failures)
  1551	                failures.extend(null_failures)
  1552	                if attribute_failures:
  1553	                    cleanup_error = BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_CLEANUP_FAILED',
  1554	                                                '|'.join(attribute_failures))
  1555	                elif null_failures:
  1556	                    cleanup_error = BrokerError('BROKER_NULL_DEVICE_CLOSE_FAILED',
  1557	                                                '|'.join(null_failures))
  1558	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  1559	            if primary is None:
  1560	                primary = error
  1561	        if cancellations and primary is None:
  1562	            # The cancellation of a cleanup is the primary error of the
  1563	            # creation when nothing else failed.  The cleanups that were
  1564	            # safe were executed before it was raised and the ordinary
  1565	            # failures that were named stay named next to it.
  1566	            primary = cancellations[0]
  1567	            failures.append('creation_cancelled:' + type(primary).__name__)
  1568	        creation = None
  1569	        if process is not None:
  1570	            # The birth query is the identity of the created process.  It
  1571	            # is independent of the cleanups above: it always runs when a
  1572	            # process was created.
  1573	            try:
