  3036	                 'alive': reader.is_alive(),
  3037	                 'error': reader.error}
  3038	        if reader.ident is None and not reader.is_alive():
  3039	            # The thread of this reader never started.  A thread that
  3040	            # never started is never joined: the join of a thread that
  3041	            # was not started is an error of the caller and not a fact
  3042	            # of the round.  The handle of this reader is closed here,
  3043	            # because no thread of the round reads it.
  3044	            state['started'] = False
  3045	            state['alive'] = False
  3046	            state['finished'] = True
  3047	            self.reader_states.append(state)
  3048	            if self._close_handle('stream', reader.handle):
  3049	                reader.handle = None
  3050	            else:
  3051	                self._retain_resource('pipe_read', reader.handle,
  3052	                                      'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
  3053	            return
  3054	        state['started'] = True
  3055	        cancelled = None
  3056	        try:
  3057	            remaining = self.remaining_cleanup_seconds()
  3058	            wait = min(READER_JOIN_SECONDS, remaining)
  3059	            if wait > 0:
  3060	                reader.finished.wait(wait)
  3061	            # The remaining budget is recomputed before the join: a wait
  3062	            # that already consumed part of the budget never receives the
  3063	            # whole budget again.
  3064	            remaining = self.remaining_cleanup_seconds()
  3065	            reader.join(min(READER_JOIN_SECONDS, remaining))
  3066	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
  3067	            # A cancellation never decides the fate of the read end: the
  3068	            # reader is asked again whether it is still alive.
  3069	            cancelled = error
  3070	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  3071	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_reader',
  3072	                                  'name': reader.name,
  3073	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__})
  3074	        alive = reader.is_alive()
  3075	        state['finished'] = reader.finished.is_set()
  3076	        state['joined'] = not alive
  3077	        state['alive'] = alive
  3078	        self.reader_states.append(state)
  3079	        if alive:
  3080	            # The reader still holds the read end.  The handle is not
  3081	            # closed under a running reader: it leaves the pending
  3082	            # table with its owner and the round keeps the fact.
  3083	            self._pending.take(reader.handle)
  3084	            self.reader_states[-1]['handle_retained'] = True
  3085	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'reader_not_stopped',
  3086	                                  'name': reader.name,
  3087	                                  'code': 'BROKER_READER_NOT_STOPPED'})
  3088	            if cancelled is not None:
  3089	                raise cancelled
  3090	            return
  3091	        if self._close_handle('stream', reader.handle):
  3092	            reader.handle = None
  3093	        else:
  3094	            self._retain_resource('pipe_read', reader.handle,
  3095	                                  'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
  3096	        if cancelled is not None:
  3097	            raise cancelled
  3098	
  3099	    def _terminate_owned(self, handle, record, reason):
  3100	        """Stop one owned process through its own original handle.
  3101	
  3102	        No pid is used and no name is used: only the handle that this core
  3103	        created is passed to the operating system.  The wait is bounded
  3104	        by the ONE cleanup deadline of the round: an expired deadline
  3105	        never starts a new wait.
  3106	
  3107	        The termination and the **confirmation of the exit** are two
  3108	        independent facts.  A termination that failed is named and it
  3109	        is preserved, and the **bounded wait** of the exit still runs
  3110	        when the original deadline of the round still permits it: a
  3111	        process whose termination failed may have exited anyway, and
  3112	        the exit is the only fact that may release its control handle.
  3113	        A failure of the termination never skips the wait and it never
  3114	        replaces it.
  3115	
  3116	        The control handle of a process is never closed because the
  3117	        termination was **requested**: only a confirmed exit may close
  3118	        it, and an expired cleanup keeps the control and it never opens
  3119	        a new budget of its own.
  3120	        """
  3121	        terminated = False
  3122	        try:
  3123	            self.win32.terminate_process(handle, 1)
  3124	            record['terminated'] = True
  3125	            terminated = True
  3126	        except BrokerError as error:
  3127	            # The original failure of the round is named and it stays
  3128	            # named: the wait of the exit below is independent of it.
  3129	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'terminate', 'reason': reason,
  3130	                                  'code': error.code})
  3131	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):
  3132	            # A cancellation is never swallowed: it leaves the method
  3133	            # and the finalizers that follow it still run.
  3134	            raise
  3135	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  3136	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'terminate', 'reason': reason,
  3137	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__})
  3138	        timeout = self._wait_timeout_ms()
  3139	        if timeout <= 0:
  3140	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'reason': reason,
  3141	                                  'code': 'BROKER_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'})
  3142	            return terminated
  3143	        try:
  3144	            record['exited'] = bool(self.win32.wait_process(handle, timeout))
  3145	        except BrokerError as error:
  3146	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'reason': reason, 'code': error.code})
  3147	        if record['exited']:
  3148	            try:
  3149	                record['exit_code'] = self.win32.exit_code(handle)
  3150	            except BrokerError as error:
  3151	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'exit_code', 'reason': reason,
  3152	                                      'code': error.code})
  3153	        return terminated
  3154	
  3155	    # -- the stop of the round ---------------------------------------------
  3156	    def stop(self, reason='STOP', *, force_unknown=False, deadline=None):
  3157	        """Stop the round and release every handle that this core holds.
  3158	
  3159	        The stop is bounded and it is idempotent: a repeated stop does not
  3160	        terminate anything twice and it does not change the evidence of
  3161	        the round.  Every handle is reported on its own, so a handle that
  3162	        could not be closed stays visible and the round becomes unknown
  3163	        instead of a silent success.
  3164	
  3165	        The steps of the stop are **independent bounded finalizers**.  The
  3166	        termination of the job, the wait for the original processes, the
  3167	        stop of the readers, the query of the job, the release of the
  3168	        original handles, the release of the job, the release of the
  3169	        pending resources and the diagnosis of the round each run in
  3170	        their own guard: a failure of one of them never skips the others.
  3171	        The first failure of the round is the one that leaves the method
  3172	        and the later failures are named next to it.
  3173	
  3174	        A stop that did not release everything does **not** latch the
  3175	        round as released: the same owner may try the remaining work
  3176	        again, inside the ONE cleanup deadline of the round.  A handle
  3177	        that was closed with a confirmed close is never closed a second
  3178	        time.
  3179	        """
  3180	        if self._released:
  3181	            return self.evidence()
  3182	        if self.state == 'STOPPING' and self._cleanup_deadline_value is not None:
  3183	            # A stop that is already running: the owner of the round does
  3184	            # not start a second one.
  3185	            return self.evidence()
  3186	        self.state = 'STOPPING'
  3187	        if self.stop_reason is None:
  3188	            self.stop_reason = reason
  3189	        # The ONE cleanup deadline of the round.  It is generated here,
  3190	        # at the first stop, and every step below uses this very value.
  3191	        deadline = self.cleanup_deadline(deadline)
  3192	        primary = None
  3193	
  3194	        def step(stage, action):
  3195	            """Run one bounded finalizer of the stop.
  3196	
  3197	            A finalizer that fails never skips the finalizers that follow
  3198	            it.  A cancellation is named as a cancellation and it is
  3199	            never turned into an ordinary evidence: it becomes the
  3200	            primary failure of the round when nothing else failed, and
  3201	            it is raised after every finalizer was executed.
  3202	            """
  3203	            nonlocal primary
  3204	            try:
  3205	                action()
  3206	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the cleanup still runs
  3207	                cancelled = isinstance(error, (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit))
  3208	                if primary is None:
  3209	                    primary = error
  3210	                self.failures.append({'stage': stage,
  3211	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__,
  3212	                                      'cancellation': cancelled})
  3213	
  3214	        step('diagnostic', self._collect_stream_counts)
  3215	        step('terminate_job', self._terminate_the_job)
  3216	        step('wait_processes', self._wait_the_processes)
  3217	        step('close_readers', self._close_readers)
  3218	        step('query_job', self._query_job)
  3219	        step('release_records', self._release_the_records)
  3220	        step('release_job', self._release_the_job)
  3221	        step('pending_resources', self._close_pending)
  3222	
  3223	        released = self._is_released()
  3224	        # The latch of a completed release is only set when the release
  3225	        # was really proven.  An unfinished cleanup never blocks the
  3226	        # recovery of the same owner.
  3227	        self._released = released
  3228	        self.state = 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED' if (force_unknown or not released) else 'RELEASED'
  3229	        if primary is not None:
  3230	            # The first failure of the round is not swallowed: the caller
  3231	            # sees it after the round was cleaned up.
  3232	            raise primary
  3233	        return self.evidence()
  3234	
  3235	    def _pending_handle_in_use(self, item):
  3236	        """Whether the owner of a pending handle is still using it.
  3237	
  3238	        The owner of a handle is an **object** and not a name: the
  3239	        reader that owns a read end is asked whether it is still alive.
  3240	        A read end that a live reader still reads from is never closed
  3241	        under it, whatever the branch that reached the pending pass did
  3242	        before.
  3243	        """
  3244	        owner = item.get('owner_ref')
  3245	        if owner is None:
  3246	            return False
  3247	        alive = getattr(owner, 'is_alive', None)
  3248	        if not callable(alive):
  3249	            return False
  3250	        try:
  3251	            return bool(alive())
  3252	        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  3253	            # A reader that cannot answer is a reader that may still be
  3254	            # alive: the handle is protected.
  3255	            return True
  3256	
  3257	    def _close_pending(self):
  3258	        """Close the handles that are still pending and name the failures.
  3259	
  3260	        The pending pass is the **last** close of the round and it may
  3261	        close a handle that the release of the records or the release of
  3262	        the job could not close.  Every handle that it really closes is
  3263	        reported to the core: the job of the round, the records of the
  3264	        round and the retained resources of the round never name a
  3265	        handle that is closed.
  3266	
  3267	        The pass never closes a handle whose owner is still using it:
  3268	        the guard is the fact of the owner and not the name of an
  3269	        owner.
  3270	        """
  3271	        retained = self._pending.close_all(self.failures,
  3272	                                           on_closed=self._note_closed_handle,
  3273	                                           protect=self._pending_handle_in_use)
  3274	        if retained:
  3275	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'pending_resources',
  3276	                                  'count': len(retained),
  3277	                                  'code': 'BROKER_PENDING_RESOURCES_RETAINED'})
  3278	
  3279	    def _note_closed_handle(self, item):
  3280	        """Learn that one handle was really closed.
  3281	
  3282	        The bookkeeping of the core has to agree with the fact of the
  3283	        close: a handle that is closed may never be named by the job of
  3284	        the round, by the records of the round or by the retained
  3285	        resources of the round.  Only the handle that was really closed
  3286	        is forgotten: a handle that could not be closed keeps its owner
  3287	        and it stays named.
  3288	
  3289	        The **owner object** of the handle is notified as well.  A
  3290	        reader whose read end was closed by the pending pass learns that
  3291	        the handle is gone: a later stop never closes the same original
  3292	        handle a second time and it never closes a handle that the
  3293	        operating system may have reused in the meantime.
  3294	        """
  3295	        handle = item['handle']
  3296	        kind = item.get('kind')
  3297	        if self.job is handle:
  3298	            self.job = None
  3299	            self.job_closed = True
  3300	        for record in self.records:
  3301	            if record.get('process_handle') is handle:
  3302	                record['process_handle_closed'] = True
  3303	                record['process_handle'] = None
  3304	            if record.get('thread_handle') is handle:
  3305	                record['thread_handle_closed'] = True
  3306	                record['thread_handle'] = None
  3307	        owner = item.get('owner_ref')
  3308	        if owner is not None and getattr(owner, 'handle', None) is handle:
  3309	            # The owner of the handle learns that it is closed.  The
  3310	            # handle of the owner is the **original** handle and it is
  3311	            # never closed twice.
  3312	            try:
  3313	                owner.handle = None
  3314	                owner.closed = True
  3315	            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  3316	                pass
  3317	        self._release_resource(kind, handle)
  3318	
  3319	    def _terminate_the_job(self):
  3320	        """Terminate the job of the round.
  3321	
  3322	        The termination happens once.  A repeated stop does not terminate
  3323	        the job of the round a second time.
  3324	        """
  3325	        if self.job is None or self.job_terminated:
  3326	            return
  3327	        try:
  3328	            self.win32.terminate_job(self.job, 1)
  3329	            self.job_terminated = True
  3330	        except BrokerError as error:
  3331	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'terminate_job', 'code': error.code})
  3332	
  3333	    def _wait_the_processes(self):
  3334	        """Wait for the original processes of the round.
  3335	
  3336	        Every process is independent of the others: a process that could
  3337	        not be waited for does not skip the wait of the next one, and an
  3338	        ordinary failure of the wait of one process does not interrupt
  3339	        the group.  The wait is bounded by the ONE cleanup deadline of
  3340	        the round and the remaining budget is recomputed before every
  3341	        single wait: an expired deadline never starts a new wait.
  3342	        """
  3343	        for record in self.records:
  3344	            try:
  3345	                self._wait_one_process(record)
  3346	            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
  3347	                # A cancellation never skips the processes that follow it:
  3348	                # it is named and it is raised after every process was
  3349	                # coordinated.
  3350	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
  3351	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__,
  3352	                                      'cancellation': True})
  3353	                self._processes_cancelled.append(error)
  3354	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  3355	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
  3356	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__})
  3357	        if self._processes_cancelled:
  3358	            raise self._processes_cancelled[0]
  3359	
  3360	    def _wait_one_process(self, record):
  3361	        """Wait for one original process of the round, on its own."""
  3362	        handle = record.get('process_handle')
  3363	        if handle is None or record['exited']:
  3364	            return
  3365	        if not record['resumed']:
  3366	            # The process never ran: it is stopped through its own
  3367	            # suspended handle.
  3368	            self._terminate_owned(handle, record, 'SUSPENDED_AT_STOP')
  3369	            return
  3370	        timeout = self._wait_timeout_ms()
  3371	        if timeout <= 0:
  3372	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
  3373	                                  'code': 'BROKER_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'})
  3374	            return
  3375	        try:
  3376	            record['exited'] = bool(self.win32.wait_process(handle, timeout))
  3377	        except BrokerError as error:
  3378	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
  3379	                                  'code': error.code})
  3380	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):
  3381	            # A cancellation is never turned into an ordinary evidence:
  3382	            # it leaves the wait of this process and it is raised after
  3383	            # every other process was coordinated.
  3384	            raise
  3385	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  3386	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
  3387	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__})
  3388	            return
  3389	        if record['exited']:
  3390	            try:
  3391	                record['exit_code'] = self.win32.exit_code(handle)
  3392	            except BrokerError as error:
  3393	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'exit_code', 'role': record['role'],
  3394	                                      'code': error.code})
  3395	            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):
  3396	                # The cancellation of the exit code is a cancellation of
  3397	                # the round and it is never swallowed by the evidence.
  3398	                raise
  3399	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  3400	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'exit_code', 'role': record['role'],
  3401	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__})
  3402	
  3403	    def _wait_timeout_ms(self):
  3404	        """The milliseconds that are left until the cleanup deadline.
  3405	
  3406	        The milliseconds are **floored** and they are never rounded
  3407	        **up**: a remainder that is smaller than the representable unit
  3408	        is zero and the wait asks for nothing.  A wait that asks for
  3409	        more than the remaining of the round outlives the round and it
  3410	        may never be used to infer that the process ended.
  3411	        """
  3412	        remaining = self.remaining_cleanup_seconds()
  3413	        if remaining <= 0:
  3414	            return 0
  3415	        return min(WAIT_MILLISECONDS, int(remaining * 1000))
  3416	
  3417	    def _release_the_records(self):
  3418	        """Close the original handles of the processes of the round.
  3419	
  3420	        A handle that was closed with a confirmed close is forgotten and
  3421	        it is never closed a second time.  A handle that could not be
  3422	        closed keeps its original reference and its owner.
  3423	
  3424	        The **control handle of a process** is closed **only** when the
  3425	        exit of that process was really confirmed.  A process whose exit
  3426	        was not confirmed - a suspended child that was never assigned to
  3427	        the job of the round, a process whose wait timed out or whose
  3428	        wait failed - may still be alive, and its control handle is the
  3429	        only handle that may stop it.  The handle therefore stays with
  3430	        the record of the process and with the pending table of the
  3431	        round, and the same owner may confirm the exit and close it
  3432	        later, inside the same cleanup deadline of the round.
  3433	
  3434	        The thread handle and the pipe handles are independent of the
  3435	        exit of the process: they are closed on their own and they never
  3436	        wait for the process.
  3437	
  3438	        Every handle of every record is closed on its own: a failure of
  3439	        one of them never skips the handles that follow it, and a
  3440	        cancellation is raised after every handle was coordinated.
  3441	        """
  3442	        cancellations = []
  3443	        for record in self.records:
  3444	            for key, kind in (('thread_handle', 'thread'),
  3445	                              ('process_handle', 'process')):
  3446	                handle = record.get(key)
  3447	                if handle is None:
  3448	                    continue
  3449	                if kind == 'process' and not self._process_eligible(record):
  3450	                    # The exit of the process was not confirmed: the
  3451	                    # control handle stays with its owner and the round
  3452	                    # keeps the fact.  The handle is never closed under
  3453	                    # a process that nobody may stop any more.
  3454	                    record[kind + '_handle_closed'] = False
  3455	                    self._retain_resource(
  3456	                        kind, handle, 'BROKER_PROCESS_EXIT_NOT_CONFIRMED')
  3457	                    self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_handle',
  3458	                                          'kind': kind,
  3459	                                          'role': record.get('role'),
  3460	                                          'code': 'BROKER_PROCESS_EXIT_NOT_CONFIRMED'})
  3461	                    continue
  3462	                try:
  3463	                    closed = self._close_handle(kind, handle)
  3464	                except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
  3465	                    # A cancellation never skips the handles that follow
  3466	                    # it: it is named and it is raised after every
  3467	                    # handle was coordinated.
  3468	                    record[kind + '_handle_closed'] = False
  3469	                    self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_handle', 'kind': kind,
  3470	                                          'role': record.get('role'),
  3471	                                          'error_type': type(error).__name__,
  3472	                                          'cancellation': True})
  3473	                    self._retain_resource(kind, handle, 'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
  3474	                    cancellations.append(error)
  3475	                    continue
  3476	                record[kind + '_handle_closed'] = closed
  3477	                if closed:
  3478	                    record[key] = None
  3479	                else:
  3480	                    self._retain_resource(kind, handle, 'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
  3481	        if cancellations:
  3482	            raise cancellations[0]
  3483	
  3484	    def _release_the_job(self):
  3485	        """Close the original handle of the job of the round.
  3486	
  3487	        A job handle that could not be closed is **not** forgotten: the
  3488	        core keeps the original reference and the round stays unknown.
  3489	        """
  3490	        if self.job is None:
  3491	            return
  3492	        handle = self.job
  3493	        closed = self._close_handle('job', handle)
  3494	        self.job_closed = closed
  3495	        if closed:
  3496	            self.job = None
  3497	            self._release_resource('job', handle)
  3498	        else:
  3499	            self._retain_resource('job', handle, 'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
  3500	
  3501	    def _is_released(self):
  3502	        """Whether the round is a proven release.
  3503	
  3504	        A release needs every fact and not only the absence of a failure:
  3505	        every owned process has to have exited, every handle has to have
  3506	        been closed, every reader has to have been joined without a read
  3507	        error and the job has to have been queried and found empty.
  3508	        """
  3509	        if self.failures:
  3510	            return False
  3511	        if not all(record['exited'] for record in self.records if record['created']):
  3512	            return False
  3513	        if not all(record['process_handle_closed'] and record['thread_handle_closed']
  3514	                   for record in self.records):
  3515	            return False
  3516	        if not self.job_closed:
  3517	            return False
  3518	        if not (self.job_active_known and self.job_active == 0):
  3519	            return False
  3520	        if not self.reader_states:
  3521	            return False
  3522	        for state in self.reader_states:
  3523	            if not state['finished'] or state['alive'] or state['error']:
  3524	                return False
  3525	        if self._pending.pending():
  3526	            return False
  3527	        return True
  3528	
  3529	    def _query_job(self):
  3530	        """Ask the job how many processes it still holds.
  3531	
  3532	        The answer is bounded.  An unknown answer and an answer that is
  3533	        over the cap are both *unknown*: neither of them is a zero.
  3534	
  3535	        The query only runs while the job handle is still open.  A
  3536	        handle that was already closed keeps the answer that it produced:
  3537	        a second stop never destroys the proof of the first one.
  3538	        """
  3539	        if self.job is None:
  3540	            return
  3541	        try:
  3542	            identifiers = self.win32.job_process_ids(self.job)
  3543	        except BrokerError as error:
  3544	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'query_job', 'code': error.code})
  3545	            self.job_active_known = False
  3546	            self.job_active = None
  3547	            return
  3548	        if len(identifiers) > MAX_DESCENDANTS:
  3549	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'query_job', 'code': 'BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT'})
  3550	            self.job_active_known = False
  3551	            self.job_active = None
  3552	            return
  3553	        self.job_active_known = True
  3554	        self.job_active = len(identifiers)
  3555	        self.job_process_ids = list(identifiers)
  3556	
  3557	    def _collect_stream_counts(self):
  3558	        total = 0
  3559	        for record in self.records:
  3560	            for stream, key in (('stdout', 'stdout_bytes'), ('stderr', 'stderr_bytes')):
  3561	                for reader in self.readers:
  3562	                    if reader.name.endswith(record['role'] + '-' + stream):
  3563	                        record[key] = reader.observed
  3564	                        record[stream + '_overflow'] = reader.overflow
  3565	                        if reader.overflow and not self._stream_failure_reported:
  3566	                            self._stream_failure_reported = True
  3567	                            self.failures.append({'stage': 'stream_limit',
  3568	                                                  'role': record['role'],
  3569	                                                  'stream': stream,
  3570	                                                  'code': 'BROKER_STREAM_LIMIT_EXCEEDED'})
  3571	        for reader in self.readers:
  3572	            total += reader.kept
  3573	        self.new_text_bytes = total
  3574	        if total > MAX_NEW_TEXT_BYTES and not self._text_failure_reported:
  3575	            self._text_failure_reported = True
  3576	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'new_text_limit',
  3577	                                  'code': 'BROKER_NEW_TEXT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED'})
  3578	
  3579	    def overflowed(self):
  3580	        """Whether a bound of the text of the children was exceeded."""
  3581	        self._collect_stream_counts()
  3582	        if self.new_text_bytes > MAX_NEW_TEXT_BYTES:
  3583	            return True
  3584	        return any(record['stdout_overflow'] or record['stderr_overflow']
  3585	                   for record in self.records)
  3586	
  3587	    def _abort_round(self, reason):
  3588	        """Fail the round closed.
  3589	
  3590	        The stop of the round is bounded and it only touches the handles
  3591	        and the job of this round.  The state of the round is unknown
  3592	        afterwards, whatever the stop itself proved.  The abort is
  3593	        idempotent: a failure that already aborted the round does not
  3594	        abort it a second time and it does not report it twice.
  3595	        """
  3596	        if not self._abort_reported:
  3597	            self._abort_reported = True
  3598	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'round', 'code': reason})
  3599	        try:
  3600	            self.stop(reason, force_unknown=True)
  3601	        except BrokerError as error:
  3602	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'abort', 'code': error.code})
  3603	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  3604	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'abort', 'error_type': type(error).__name__})
  3605	        self.state = 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
  3606	
  3607	    # -- the evidence ------------------------------------------------------
  3608	    def summary(self):
  3609	        """The bounded summary of the round that fits into one frame."""
  3610	        return {'state': self.state,
  3611	                'roles': [{'role': record['role'], 'pid': record['pid'],
  3612	                           'created': record['created'], 'assigned': record['assigned'],
  3613	                           'resumed': record['resumed'], 'exited': record['exited']}
  3614	                          for record in self.records],
  3615	                'job_active_known': self.job_active_known,
  3616	                'job_active': self.job_active,
  3617	                'failures': len(self.failures)}
  3618	
  3619	    def evidence(self):
  3620	        return {
  3621	            'kind': 'lvw.s3-native-broker-evidence/1',
  3622	            'state': self.state,
  3623	            'run_id': self.plan.run_id,
  3624	            'candidate_sha': self.plan.candidate_sha,
  3625	            'authorization_sha256': self.plan.authorization_sha256,
  3626	            'grant_authority': 'NOT_VERIFIED',
  3627	            'stop_reason': self.stop_reason,
  3628	            'broker': self.broker_identity,
  3629	            'job': {'created': self.job is not None or self.job_closed,
  3630	                    'kill_on_job_close': self.job_kill_on_close,
  3631	                    'breakaway_allowed': False,
  3632	                    'terminated': self.job_terminated,
  3633	                    'active_process_count': self.job_active,
  3634	                    'active_process_count_known': self.job_active_known,
  3635	                    'process_ids': list(self.job_process_ids),
  3636	                    'handle_closed': self.job_closed},
  3637	            'roles': [{key: value for key, value in record.items()
  3638	                       if key not in ('process_handle', 'thread_handle')}
  3639	                      for record in self.records],
  3640	            'new_text_bytes': self.new_text_bytes,
  3641	            'readers': list(self.reader_states),
  3642	            'pending_resources': self._pending.pending_facts(),
  3643	            # The resources that this round could not release and the
  3644	            # original handles that the Windows façade still holds.  They
  3645	            # are named with their original references: a resource that
  3646	            # was not released is never replaced by a copy of a message.
  3647	            'retained_resources': [{'kind': item['kind'], 'owner': item['owner'],
  3648	                                    'code': item['code'],
  3649	                                    'handle_repr': repr(item['handle'])}
  3650	                                   for item in self._retained_resources],
  3651	            'api_retained': self._api_retained(),
  3652	            'cleanup_deadline': self._cleanup_deadline_value,
  3653	            'failures': list(self.failures),
  3654	            # The claims that this package does not make.
  3655	            'native_execution_proven': False,
  3656	            'native_cleanup_proven': False,
  3657	            'gpu_released_proven': False,
  3658	            'port_released_proven': False,
  3659	            'sampler_proven': False,
  3660	            'parent_crash_recovery_proven': False,
  3661	            'windows_stdin_eof_proven': False,
  3662	            'admission_produced': False,
  3663	            'stage_pass_claim': False,
  3664	            'product_gate0': False,
  3665	        }
  3666	
  3667	
  3668	# -- the bounded private pipe ----------------------------------------------
  3669	
  3670	def _require_deadline(deadline, code='BROKER_DEADLINE_INVALID'):
  3671	    """Check one absolute deadline.
  3672	
  3673	    A deadline is a finite number of seconds of the monotonic clock of
  3674	    the host.  A boolean, a string, an infinite value and a value that
  3675	    is not a number at all are refused: a deadline that was understood
  3676	    as something else would silently unbound the round.
  3677	    """
  3678	    if deadline is None:
  3679	        return None
  3680	    if isinstance(deadline, bool) or not isinstance(deadline, (int, float)):
  3681	        raise BrokerError(code)
  3682	    value = float(deadline)
  3683	    if not math.isfinite(value):
  3684	        raise BrokerError(code)
  3685	    return value
  3686	
  3687	
  3688	class _PosixSyscalls:
  3689	    """The POSIX system calls of the private pipe.
  3690	
  3691	    The table is a narrow and explicit interface.  It is the only place
  3692	    where the private channel touches the operating system, and it can
  3693	    be replaced by a synthetic table in the offline validation: the
  3694	    production logic of the channel is then driven by a table that
  3695	    records the calls instead of by the kernel.
  3696	    """
  3697	
  3698	    def __init__(self):
  3699	        self._pollers = {}
  3700	
  3701	    def fileno(self, file_object):
  3702	        return file_object.fileno()
  3703	
  3704	    def set_blocking(self, descriptor, blocking):
  3705	        os.set_blocking(descriptor, blocking)
  3706	
  3707	    def _poller(self, descriptor):
  3708	        poller = self._pollers.get(descriptor)
  3709	        if poller is None:
  3710	            poller = select.poll()
  3711	            poller.register(descriptor,
  3712	                            select.POLLIN | select.POLLOUT
  3713	                            | select.POLLERR | select.POLLHUP)
  3714	            self._pollers[descriptor] = poller
  3715	        return poller
  3716	
  3717	    def poll(self, descriptor, mask, timeout):
  3718	        """Whether the descriptor is ready for the requested operation."""
  3719	        ready = self._poller(descriptor).poll(max(0.0, timeout) * 1000)
  3720	        for number, event in ready:
  3721	            if event & select.POLLNVAL:
  3722	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_DESCRIPTOR_INVALID')
  3723	            if mask == 'read' and event & (select.POLLIN | select.POLLHUP
  3724	                                           | select.POLLERR):
  3725	                return True
  3726	            if mask == 'write' and event & (select.POLLOUT | select.POLLHUP
  3727	                                            | select.POLLERR):
  3728	                return True
  3729	        return False
  3730	
  3731	    def read(self, descriptor, size):
  3732	        return os.read(descriptor, size)
  3733	
  3734	    def write(self, descriptor, data):
  3735	        return os.write(descriptor, data)
  3736	
  3737	    def close(self, file_object):
  3738	        file_object.close()
  3739	
  3740	
  3741	class _PosixPipeEndpoint:
  3742	    """One end of a private pipe on a POSIX host.
  3743	
  3744	    The endpoint owns the original descriptor of the pipe and it closes
  3745	    it itself.  The descriptor is set to non blocking exactly once, by
  3746	    the owner that created it, and the read and the write poll it in
  3747	    slices of at most 50 milliseconds.  Every slice checks the cancel
  3748	    flag and the absolute deadline, so that a peer that never reads can
  3749	    never hold the supervisor of the round.
  3750	
  3751	    The endpoint never changes the blocking mode of a descriptor that it
  3752	    does not own and it never closes a descriptor that it does not own.
  3753	    It never relies on the lock of a buffered object either: the close
  3754	    of a buffered object may wait for a reader that holds its lock, and
  3755	    the close of this endpoint is the close of the owner of the
  3756	    descriptor.
  3757	    """
  3758	
  3759	    def __init__(self, file_object, *, name, syscalls=None, clock=None):
  3760	        self._file = file_object
  3761	        self._name = name
  3762	        self._syscalls = syscalls or _PosixSyscalls()
  3763	        self._clock = clock or time.monotonic
  3764	        self._descriptor = self._syscalls.fileno(file_object)
  3765	        self._non_blocking = False
  3766	        self._closed = False
  3767	        # The absolute deadline that is bound to this endpoint.  It may
  3768	        # only shorten the deadline that is already bound and it is read
  3769	        # again on every slice: a deadline that was bound after a read
  3770	        # started still tightens that read.
  3771	        self._deadline = None
  3772	        self.facts = {'name': name, 'owner': 'POSIX_ENDPOINT',
  3773	                      'descriptor': self._descriptor}
  3774	
  3775	    def bind_deadline(self, deadline):
  3776	        """Bind an absolute deadline to this endpoint.
  3777	
  3778	        The deadline may only shorten the deadline that is already
  3779	        bound.  It is a property of the endpoint and not a local
  3780	        argument of one call: a call that passed ``None`` before the
  3781	        binding still observes the bound deadline.
  3782	        """
  3783	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
  3784	        if value is None:
  3785	            return self._deadline
  3786	        if self._deadline is not None and value > self._deadline:
  3787	            return self._deadline
  3788	        self._deadline = value
  3789	        return value
  3790	
  3791	    def _effective_deadline(self, deadline=None):
  3792	        """The deadline that this call really uses."""
  3793	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
  3794	        if self._deadline is None:
  3795	            return value
  3796	        if value is None:
  3797	            return self._deadline
  3798	        return min(value, self._deadline)
  3799	
  3800	    def prepare(self):
  3801	        """Set the descriptor to non blocking, once, by its owner."""
  3802	        if self._non_blocking or self._closed:
  3803	            return
  3804	        self._syscalls.set_blocking(self._descriptor, False)
  3805	        self._non_blocking = True
  3806	        self.facts['non_blocking'] = True
  3807	
  3808	    def _check(self, deadline, cancel):
  3809	        if cancel is not None and cancel.is_set():
  3810	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED', self._name)
  3811	        if deadline is not None and self._clock() >= deadline:
  3812	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT', self._name)
  3813	
  3814	    def read(self, deadline=None, cancel=None):
  3815	        """Read one block of the pipe, bounded by the deadline."""
  3816	        self.prepare()
  3817	        while True:
  3818	            self._check(self._effective_deadline(deadline), cancel)
  3819	            if not self._syscalls.poll(self._descriptor, 'read',
  3820	                                       POLL_SLICE_SECONDS):
  3821	                continue
  3822	            try:
  3823	                data = self._syscalls.read(self._descriptor, PIPE_READ_BYTES)
  3824	            except BlockingIOError:
  3825	                # The pipe was not ready after all: the slice is repeated.
  3826	                continue
  3827	            except OSError as error:
  3828	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_READ_FAILED',
  3829	                                  '%s:%s' % (self._name, type(error).__name__))
  3830	            if not data:
  3831	                return b''
  3832	            return data
  3833	
  3834	    def write(self, data, deadline=None, cancel=None):
  3835	        """Write the whole block, bounded by the deadline."""
  3836	        self.prepare()
  3837	        view = memoryview(bytes(data))
  3838	        total = 0
  3839	        while total < len(view):
  3840	            self._check(self._effective_deadline(deadline), cancel)
  3841	            if not self._syscalls.poll(self._descriptor, 'write',
  3842	                                       POLL_SLICE_SECONDS):
  3843	                continue
  3844	            try:
  3845	                count = self._syscalls.write(self._descriptor, view[total:])
  3846	            except BlockingIOError:
  3847	                # The pipe was full after all: the slice is repeated.
  3848	                continue
  3849	            except OSError as error:
  3850	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_WRITE_FAILED',
  3851	                                  '%s:%s' % (self._name, type(error).__name__))
  3852	            if count <= 0:
  3853	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_WRITE_FAILED', self._name)
  3854	            total += count
  3855	        return total
  3856	
  3857	    def close(self):
  3858	        """Close the original descriptor of this endpoint, exactly once.
  3859	
  3860	        The endpoint is closed **only** when the close really happened.
  3861	        A close that failed keeps the descriptor and its owner: the
  3862	        endpoint may try it again and it is never marked as closed
  3863	        irreversibly.
  3864	
  3865	        A close of an endpoint that is already closed keeps the fact
  3866	        that it is closed: the second close is idempotent and it never
  3867	        turns a proven close into a missing one.
  3868	        """
  3869	        if self._closed:
  3870	            self.facts['closed'] = True
  3871	            self.facts['already_closed'] = True
  3872	            self.facts['retained_workers'] = 0
  3873	            self.facts['worker_thread_handle_released'] = True
  3874	            return self.facts
  3875	        try:
  3876	            self._syscalls.close(self._file)
  3877	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  3878	            self.facts['closed'] = False
  3879	            self.facts['error_type'] = type(error).__name__
  3880	            self.facts['retained_workers'] = 0
  3881	            self.facts['worker_thread_handle_released'] = True
  3882	            return self.facts
  3883	        self._closed = True
  3884	        self.facts['closed'] = True
  3885	        # This host has no worker thread for an end of the pipe: the
  3886	        # blocking calls are made by the reader of the channel itself.
  3887	        # There is therefore no original worker handle that could be
  3888	        # outstanding, and the fact says so.
  3889	        self.facts['retained_workers'] = 0
  3890	        self.facts['worker_thread_handle_released'] = True
  3891	        return self.facts
  3892	
  3893	
  3894	class _WindowsPipeWorker(threading.Thread):
  3895	    """One worker of one end of a private pipe on a Windows host.
  3896	
  3897	    The worker owns one blocking operation at a time.  It takes the
  3898	    request from its queue, it performs the blocking call on the
  3899	    original handle of the pipe and it reports the real completion of
  3900	    the call.  The thread is created once for the endpoint and it is
  3901	    never created for a frame.
  3902	
  3903	    The handle of the thread is the handle of the thread itself and
  3904	    never a self reported identifier: ``CancelSynchronousIo`` needs the
  3905	    original handle of the thread that performs the operation.
  3906	
  3907	    The lifecycle of the worker is **explicit**.  The states are
  3908	    ``IDLE``, ``QUEUED``, ``IN_IO``, ``CANCELLING``, ``DONE`` and
  3909	    ``STOPPED``:
  3910	
  3911	    * ``IDLE``      the worker waits for a request;
  3912	    * ``QUEUED``    a request waits for the worker;
  3913	    * ``IN_IO``     the worker is inside the blocking call;
  3914	    * ``CANCELLING`` a cancellation was asked for;
  3915	    * ``DONE``      the request of the worker completed;
  3916	    * ``STOPPED``   the thread of the worker really ended.
  3917	
  3918	    ``DONE`` is not ``STOPPED``: the completion of a request says
  3919	    nothing about the thread of the worker, and the handle of the
  3920	    thread may only be closed after the thread really ended.
  3921	
  3922	    The worker is the **only** owner of the handle of its own thread:
  3923	    it duplicated that handle and it closes it when its thread really
  3924	    ended.  A worker that did not stop keeps its handle and its owner:
  3925	    the handle is never closed under a live worker and it is never left
  3926	    to the garbage collector.
  3927	    """
  3928	
  3929	    STATES = ('IDLE', 'QUEUED', 'IN_IO', 'CANCELLING', 'DONE', 'STOPPED')
  3930	
  3931	    def __init__(self, api, handle, *, kind, action, name, clock=None,
  3932	                 context=None):
  3933	        super().__init__(daemon=True, name=name)
  3934	        self.api = api
  3935	        self.handle = handle
  3936	        self.kind = kind
  3937	        self.action = action
  3938	        self._clock = clock or time.monotonic
  3939	        self._requests = queue.Queue(maxsize=1)
  3940	        # The event of the in-flight operation.  It is set while the
  3941	        # worker is inside the call and it is cleared again when the
  3942	        # call ended: an event that is never cleared would report a
  3943	        # permanent in-flight state.
  3944	        self.inside = threading.Event()
  3945	        self.done = threading.Event()
  3946	        self.stopped = threading.Event()
  3947	        self.ready = threading.Event()
  3948	        self.result = None
  3949	        self.error = None
  3950	        # The **original** error object of the last request.  The code
  3951	        # of the error is a name; the object is the failure.  It is kept
  3952	        # so that the endpoint may raise the very object that the
  3953	        # primitive raised instead of a copy of its name.
  3954	        self._error_object = None
  3955	        self.thread_handle = None
  3956	        self.thread_handle_released = False
  3957	        self.joined = False
  3958	        self.state = 'IDLE'
  3959	        # The number of the requests that were submitted and the number
  3960	        # of the requests that completed.  The two numbers are the real
  3961	        # completion of a request: the event alone could report the
  3962	        # completion of an older request.
  3963	        self._condition = threading.Condition()
  3964	        self._submitted = 0
  3965	        self._completed = 0
  3966	        # The generations of the requests that were handed over, in the
  3967	        # order in which they were handed over.  The worker takes the
  3968	        # request and its generation together: a worker that would read
  3969	        # the number of the submissions would name the generation of a
  3970	        # later request.
  3971	        self._generations = collections.deque()
  3972	        # The **outcome of every generation**.  The outcome carries the
  3973	        # result, the error, the **original error object**, the source
  3974	        # of the failure and the order of the completion.  A result
  3975	        # that was really published is never replaced by a later one and
  3976	        # a cancellation never rewrites the outcome of a generation.
  3977	        self._outcomes = {}
  3978	        # The **admission guard of every generation**: the cancel flag
  3979	        # and the absolute deadline that the request of the generation
  3980	        # carries.  The guard is the admission of the worker itself: a
  3981	        # request whose cancel was published or whose deadline arrived
  3982	        # **before** the worker entered the operation performs **no**
  3983	        # primitive call at all.  The guard travels with the request
  3984	        # and it is never a deadline of the worker.
  3985	        self._guards = {}
  3986	        # The explicit notification that a **failure** of a generation
  3987	        # was really published.  It is set under the lock of the worker
  3988	        # and it is set **after** the outcome was recorded: an owner
  3989	        # that waits for it knows that the failure of the round is
  3990	        # published and that a later cancellation may not replace it.
  3991	        self.outcome_published = threading.Event()
  3992	        # The order of the events of this worker.  Every completion and
  3993	        # every cancellation takes a number of it, under the lock of the
  3994	        # worker: the number tells whether a failure was published
  3995	        # **before** the cancellation of the round or after it.
  3996	        self._sequence = 0
  3997	        self._cancel_sequence = None
  3998	        # The ONE context of the round.  The worker reads the absolute
  3999	        # deadline and the published cleanup deadline of the round from
  4000	        # it and it never keeps a budget of its own.
  4001	        self._context = context
  4002	        # The flag of the cancellation.  It stops the worker from
  4003	        # accepting a new task before the cancellation is requested.
  4004	        self._cancelled = False
  4005	        # The name of the cancel flag is not ``_stop``: the base class of
  4006	        # the thread uses that name for its own method and a shadow of it
  4007	        # would break the join of the thread.
  4008	        self._stop_event = threading.Event()
  4009	        # The pause of the retry of a cancellation.  It is its **own**
  4010	        # event and it is never set: the retry of a cancellation may not
  4011	        # wait on the stop event of the worker, because the worker set
  4012	        # that event itself and the wait would return at once.
  4013	        self._pause = threading.Event()
  4014	
  4015	    # -- the lifecycle ----------------------------------------------------
  4016	    def run(self):
  4017	        try:
  4018	            self.thread_handle = self.api.duplicate_current_thread_handle()
  4019	        except BrokerError as error:
  4020	            self.error = error.code
  4021	            self.done.set()
  4022	            self.ready.set()
  4023	            self.state = 'DONE'
  4024	            self.stopped.set()
  4025	            return
  4026	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  4027	            self.error = type(error).__name__
  4028	            self.done.set()
  4029	            self.ready.set()
  4030	            self.state = 'DONE'
  4031	            self.stopped.set()
  4032	            return
  4033	        self.ready.set()
  4034	        try:
  4035	            while not self._stop_event.is_set():
  4036	                try:
  4037	                    request = self._requests.get(timeout=0.1)
  4038	                except queue.Empty:
  4039	                    continue
  4040	                with self._condition:
  4041	                    generation = (self._generations.popleft()
  4042	                                  if self._generations else self._submitted)
  4043	                self.error = None
  4044	                self.result = None
  4045	                self._error_object = None
  4046	                # The check of the cancellation and the publication of
  4047	                # the intent to enter the operation are **one** atomic
  4048	                # step under the lock of the worker.  A cancellation
  4049	                # that arrives between the check and the operation would
  4050	                # otherwise be answered by a worker that performs the
  4051	                # operation anyway: the cancellation and the operation
  4052	                # would be two uncoordinated facts.
  4053	                #
  4054	                # The **admission guard of the generation** is asked in
  4055	                # the same atomic step: the cancel flag and the
  4056	                # deadline of the request travel with the request and
  4057	                # the worker never enters the operation of a request
  4058	                # whose admission was already refused.
  4059	                with self._condition:
  4060	                    cancelled = self._cancelled
  4061	                    if not cancelled:
  4062	                        guard = self._guards.get(generation)
  4063	                        if guard is not None:
  4064	                            guard_cancel, guard_deadline = guard
  4065	                            # The **current** original context of the
  4066	                            # round is consulted at the moment of the
  4067	                            # decision of the dispatch.  A cancellation
  4068	                            # or an expiry that was published **before**
  4069	                            # this decision refuses the admission and
  4070	                            # the primitive is never called at all: the
  4071	                            # captured request of the call is never the
  4072	                            # authority of the decision.
  4073	                            context = self._context
  4074	                            if context is not None:
  4075	                                if context.cancel.is_set():
  4076	                                    cancelled = True
  4077	                                    self.error = 'BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED'
  4078	                                    self._error_object = _cancel_source_error(
  4079	                                        BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED',
  4080	                                                    self.kind), context.cancel)
  4081	                                else:
  4082	                                    # The effective minimum is the one
  4083	                                    # of the context: the bound of the
  4084	                                    # call, the **current** absolute
  4085	                                    # deadline of the round and the
  4086	                                    # **published** cleanup deadline.
  4087	                                    current = self._effective_deadline(
  4088	                                        guard_deadline)
  4089	                                    if (current is not None
  4090	                                            and self._clock() >= current):
  4091	                                        cancelled = True
  4092	                                        self.error = 'BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT'
  4093	                                        self._error_object = BrokerError(
  4094	                                            'BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT', self.kind)
  4095	                            if (not cancelled and guard_cancel is not None
  4096	                                    and guard_cancel.is_set()):
  4097	                                cancelled = True
  4098	                                self.error = 'BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED'
  4099	                                # The error of the refusal carries the
  4100	                                # owner of the cancel flag, exactly like
  4101	                                # the refusal of the endpoint: a
  4102	                                # cancellation that the close of the
  4103	                                # channel initiated is a named fact of
  4104	                                # that close and it is never the error
  4105	                                # of the reader.
  4106	                                self._error_object = _cancel_source_error(
  4107	                                    BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED',
  4108	                                                self.kind), guard_cancel)
  4109	                            elif (not cancelled and guard_deadline is not None
  4110	                                  and self._clock() >= guard_deadline):
  4111	                                cancelled = True
  4112	                                self.error = 'BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT'
  4113	                                self._error_object = BrokerError(
  4114	                                    'BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT', self.kind)
  4115	                    if not cancelled:
  4116	                        self.state = 'IN_IO'
  4117	                        self.inside.set()
  4118	                if cancelled:
  4119	                    # The cancellation reached the worker before the
  4120	                    # operation started: the operation is not performed
  4121	                    # at all and the request is answered as finished.
  4122	                    self._complete(generation)
  4123	                    continue
  4124	                try:
  4125	                    self.result = self.action(request)
  4126	                except BrokerError as error:
  4127	                    self.error = error.code
  4128	                    self._error_object = error
  4129	                except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  4130	                    self.error = type(error).__name__
  4131	                    self._error_object = error
  4132	                finally:
  4133	                    self.inside.clear()
  4134	                    self._complete(generation)
  4135	        finally:
  4136	            self.state = 'STOPPED'
  4137	            self.stopped.set()
  4138	            # The worker is the only owner of the handle of its own
  4139	            # thread and it closes it here, when its thread really ended.
  4140	            self.release_thread_handle()
  4141	
  4142	    def _complete(self, generation=None):
  4143	        """Report the real completion of the current request.
  4144	
  4145	        The outcome of the generation is **recorded** here, under the
  4146	        lock of the worker: the result, the code of the error, the
  4147	        **original error object** and the order of the completion.  The
  4148	        record is the history of the request and it is never replaced
  4149	        by a later one.
  4150	
  4151	        The order of the completion is the order of the events of the
  4152	        worker: it tells whether a failure was published **before** the
  4153	        cancellation of the round or after it.  A failure that was
  4154	        published before the cancellation is a fault of the round and it
  4155	        stays the first error; a failure that was published after the
  4156	        cancellation is the consequence of the cancellation.
  4157	        """
  4158	        with self._condition:
  4159	            self._completed += 1
  4160	            self._sequence += 1
  4161	            if generation is not None:
  4162	                record = {
  4163	                    'generation': generation,
  4164	                    'result': self.result,
  4165	                    'error': self.error,
  4166	                    'error_object': self._error_object,
  4167	                    'source': 'WORKER',
  4168	                    'sequence': self._sequence,
  4169	                    'cancel_sequence': self._cancel_sequence,
  4170	                }
  4171	                self._outcomes[generation] = record
  4172	                if record['error'] is not None:
  4173	                    # The failure of this generation is published: a
  4174	                    # later cancellation of the round may not replace
  4175	                    # it.
  4176	                    self.outcome_published.set()
  4177	            self._condition.notify_all()
  4178	        self.done.set()
  4179	        self.state = 'DONE'
  4180	
  4181	    def outcome(self, generation):
  4182	        """The recorded outcome of one generation of this worker.
  4183	
  4184	        The outcome is the **original** outcome of the request: the
  4185	        result, the error and the error object of the request that
  4186	        really ran.  It is ``None`` when the request did not complete.
  4187	        """
  4188	        with self._condition:
  4189	            record = self._outcomes.get(generation)
  4190	            return dict(record) if record is not None else None
  4191	
  4192	    def published_before_cancel(self, generation):
  4193	        """Whether the outcome of this generation was published first.
  4194	
  4195	        The comparison of the two orders is the only proof that a
  4196	        failure of the round happened **before** the cancellation of the
  4197	        round.  A cancellation that was requested first may not rewrite
  4198	        the failure of a request that was still running, and a failure
  4199	        that was published first may never be replaced by a
  4200	        cancellation.
  4201	
  4202	        A worker that was never cancelled proves the order by itself:
  4203	        the failure was published before any cancellation of it.
  4204	        """
  4205	        with self._condition:
  4206	            record = self._outcomes.get(generation)
  4207	            if record is None or record.get('error') is None:
  4208	                return False
  4209	            if self._cancel_sequence is None:
  4210	                return True
  4211	            return record['sequence'] < self._cancel_sequence
  4212	
  4213	    def _request_cancellation(self):
  4214	        """Take the order of the cancellation of this worker.
  4215	
  4216	        The order is taken under the lock of the worker, so that the
  4217	        comparison with the order of a completion is a real order and
  4218	        not a guess of the scheduler.
  4219	        """
  4220	        with self._condition:
  4221	            self._cancelled = True
  4222	            if self._cancel_sequence is None:
  4223	                self._sequence += 1
  4224	                self._cancel_sequence = self._sequence
  4225	            self.state = 'CANCELLING'
  4226	            self._condition.notify_all()
  4227	        self._stop_event.set()
  4228	
  4229	    def wait_ready(self, seconds):
  4230	        """Wait for the worker to have acquired the handle of its thread."""
  4231	        return self.ready.wait(max(0.0, seconds))
  4232	
  4233	    def submit(self, request, cancel=None, deadline=None):
  4234	        """Hand one request to the worker.
  4235	
  4236	        The hand over is bounded: the queue of the worker holds one
  4237	        request and a request that does not fit is refused with a named
  4238	        error.  A worker that is dead and a worker whose thread handle
  4239	        could not be created refuse the request as well.
  4240	
  4241	        The hand over and the cancellation share **one** lock: a
  4242	        request that is submitted after the cancellation of the worker
  4243	        is refused and it never enters the queue.  The cancellation and
  4244	        the submission are therefore coordinated by the same generation
  4245	        of the worker and a request of an older generation is never
  4246	        taken for the completion of this one.
  4247	
  4248	        The **admission guard** of the request - the cancel flag and the
  4249	        absolute deadline of the call - travels with the request: the
  4250	        worker asks it before it enters the operation and a request
  4251	        whose admission was refused performs **no** primitive call.
  4252	
  4253	        The completion of a request is the number of the completed
  4254	        requests and not the event alone: the completion of an older
  4255	        request is never the completion of this one.
  4256	        """
  4257	        if not self.is_alive():
  4258	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_NOT_ALIVE', self.kind)
  4259	        if self.thread_handle is None:
  4260	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_NOT_READY',
  4261	                               '%s:%s' % (self.kind, self.error))
  4262	        with self._condition:
  4263	            if self._cancelled:
  4264	                # The cancellation of this worker arrived first: the
  4265	                # request is refused and it never enters the queue.
  4266	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_CANCELLED', self.kind)
  4267	            self._submitted += 1
  4268	            generation = self._submitted
  4269	            # The generation travels with the request: the worker takes
  4270	            # the request and the generation of **that** request.  The
  4271	            # generation is registered **before** the request is handed
  4272	            # over: a worker that takes the request never finds an
  4273	            # empty list of generations.
  4274	            self._generations.append(generation)
  4275	            self._guards[generation] = (cancel, deadline)
  4276	            try:
  4277	                self._requests.put(request, timeout=POLL_SLICE_SECONDS)
  4278	            except queue.Full:
  4279	                self._submitted -= 1
  4280	                self._guards.pop(generation, None)
  4281	                if self._generations and self._generations[-1] == generation:
  4282	                    self._generations.pop()
  4283	                self._condition.notify_all()
  4284	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_QUEUE_FULL', self.kind)
  4285	            self.done.clear()
  4286	            self.state = 'QUEUED'
  4287	            return generation
  4288	
  4289	    def wait_done(self, generation, timeout):
  4290	        """Wait for the completion of one request, bounded."""
  4291	        with self._condition:
  4292	            return self._condition.wait_for(
  4293	                lambda: self._completed >= generation, max(0.0, timeout))
  4294	
  4295	    def request_cancel(self, deadline=None):
  4296	        """Ask for the cancellation of the operation of this worker.
  4297	
  4298	        The request is not a completion.  The caller has to wait for the
  4299	        real completion of the worker and to join it.
  4300	
  4301	        The cancellation stops the worker from accepting a new task
  4302	        **first** and it drives the worker to its end: the worker that
  4303	        is inside an operation is asked to leave it and the worker that
  4304	        waits for a request is asked to stop waiting.  A cancellation
  4305	        that reports ``NOT_FOUND`` is repeated, bounded by the shared
  4306	        deadline, until the worker reports that **this** generation is
  4307	        done or that the thread really stopped: a cancellation that was
  4308	        sent once and dropped is not a cancellation.
  4309	
  4310	        The retry never waits on the stop event of the worker: the
  4311	        worker set that event itself, a moment before, and a wait that
  4312	        returns at once would end the retry after a single
  4313	        ``NOT_FOUND``.  The retry waits on its own pause instead, so
  4314	        that the request of the operating system is really repeated.
  4315	
  4316	        The completion of an **older** generation never releases the
  4317	        retry: the real completion of the request of this generation is
  4318	        the number of the completed requests.
  4319	
  4320	        The deadline of the retry is **recomputed on every round** of
  4321	        the loop: a deadline that was published after the retry started
  4322	        still shortens it, and the pause of the retry is cropped to the
  4323	        time that is really left.  A retry that used the number of the
  4324	        deadline that it found when it started would wait longer than
  4325	        the round allows.
  4326	        """
  4327	        self._request_cancellation()
  4328	        if self.thread_handle is None:
  4329	            return 'NO_HANDLE'
  4330	        # The bound of the retry is fixed **once**: a retry that used a
  4331	        # deadline that moved with every round would never expire.
  4332	        bound = self._effective_deadline(deadline)
  4333	        if bound is None:
  4334	            # The retry of a cancellation is **always** bounded: a
  4335	            # worker that never ends may not hold the caller for ever.
  4336	            # The bound of the package is used only when neither the
  4337	            # caller nor the context of the round brought a deadline.
  4338	            bound = self._clock() + CLOSE_JOIN_SECONDS
  4339	        while True:
  4340	            try:
  4341	                state = self.api.cancel_synchronous_io(self.thread_handle)
  4342	            except BrokerError as error:
  4343	                self.error = error.code
  4344	                return 'FAILED'
  4345	            except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  4346	                self.error = type(error).__name__
  4347	                return 'FAILED'
  4348	            if state != 'NOT_FOUND':
  4349	                return state
  4350	            # The operation was not found: it had not started yet or it
  4351	            # had just finished.  The worker is asked again until the
  4352	            # request of this generation completed or until the thread
  4353	            # of the worker really stopped.
  4354	            with self._condition:
  4355	                completed = self._completed >= self._submitted
  4356	            if completed or self.stopped.is_set():
  4357	                return 'NOT_FOUND'
  4358	            # The deadline of this round of the retry is read **again**
  4359	            # from the call and from the context of the round: a
  4360	            # deadline that was published after the retry started still
  4361	            # shortens the retry that is running.
  4362	            effective = self._effective_deadline(deadline)
  4363	            if effective is None or bound < effective:
  4364	                effective = bound
  4365	            if self._clock() >= effective:
  4366	                return 'NOT_FOUND'
  4367	            # The pause of the retry is its own event: the stop event of
  4368	            # the worker was set by this very method and it may never be
  4369	            # used as the sleep of the retry.  The pause is cropped to
  4370	            # the time that is really left.
  4371	            pause = POLL_SLICE_SECONDS
  4372	            if effective is not None:
  4373	                pause = max(0.0, min(pause, effective - self._clock()))
  4374	            if pause <= 0:
  4375	                return 'NOT_FOUND'
  4376	            self._pause.wait(pause)
  4377	
  4378	    def _effective_deadline(self, call=None):
  4379	        """The ONE deadline that a wait of this worker really uses.
  4380	
  4381	        The deadline of the call, the absolute deadline of the round and
  4382	        the **published** cleanup deadline of the round are one value:
  4383	        the earliest of them.  The value is recomputed on every call.
  4384	        """
  4385	        value = _require_deadline(call)
  4386	        context = self._context
  4387	        if context is not None:
  4388	            absolute = context.absolute_deadline
  4389	            if absolute is not None and (value is None or absolute < value):
  4390	                value = absolute
  4391	            cleanup = context.cleanup_deadline_value()
  4392	            if cleanup is not None and (value is None or cleanup < value):
  4393	                value = cleanup
  4394	        return value
  4395	
  4396	    def stop(self):
  4397	        """Ask the worker to end its loop."""
  4398	        self._stop_event.set()
  4399	
  4400	    def stop_accepting(self):
  4401	        """Stop the worker from accepting a new task.
  4402	
  4403	        The flag is written under the **same** lock that the worker of
  4404	        the endpoint reads it under: the cancellation and the intent to
  4405	        enter the operation are one atomic step.
  4406	
  4407	        The **order** of the cancellation is taken here as well: a
  4408	        failure that was published before this call is a fault of the
  4409	        round and a cancellation may never replace it.
  4410	        """
  4411	        self._request_cancellation()
  4412	
  4413	    def release_thread_handle(self):
  4414	        """Close the handle of the thread of this worker.
  4415	
  4416	        The worker is the only owner of that handle.  It is closed here
  4417	        and only here, and only after the thread of the worker really
  4418	        ended.  A worker that did not stop keeps its handle.
  4419	        """
  4420	        if self.thread_handle is None:
  4421	            return True
  4422	        handle = self.thread_handle
  4423	        try:
  4424	            self.api.close_handle(handle)
  4425	        except BrokerError as error:
  4426	            self.error = error.code
  4427	            return False
  4428	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
  4429	            self.error = type(error).__name__
  4430	            return False
  4431	        self.thread_handle = None
  4432	        self.thread_handle_released = True
  4433	        return True
  4434	
  4435	    def join_bounded(self, seconds):
  4436	        """Join the worker and report whether it really stopped.
  4437	
  4438	        A worker whose thread was never started cannot be joined: the
  4439	        join of such a thread is an error of the caller and not a fact
  4440	        of the round.  The join reports that the worker did not stop
  4441	        and it never pretends that the thread ended.
  4442	        """
  4443	        try:
  4444	            self.join(max(0.0, seconds))
  4445	        except RuntimeError:
  4446	            # The thread was never started: it is not a thread of the
  4447	            # round that ended, it is a thread that never came up.
  4448	            self.joined = False
  4449	            return False
  4450	        self.joined = not self.is_alive()
  4451	        return self.joined
  4452	
  4453	
  4454	class _WindowsPipeEndpoint:
  4455	    """One end of a private pipe on a Windows host.
  4456	
  4457	    The read and the write of the endpoint each own exactly one worker
  4458	    thread and there is never more than one of them: the number of the
  4459	    threads of a channel is fixed and it never grows with the number of
  4460	    the frames.
  4461	
  4462	    The supervisor never performs the blocking call itself.  It waits
  4463	    for the completion of the worker in bounded slices, it checks the
  4464	    cancel flag and the deadline on every slice and it cancels the
  4465	    worker through the original handle of its thread when the deadline
  4466	    arrives.  The worker that is inside the call is the one that may
  4467	    block; the supervisor never does.
  4468	
  4469	    The cancellation of a synchronous operation is a request and not a
  4470	    completion: ``CancelSynchronousIo`` may report that the operation
  4471	    was not found because it had not started yet or because it had just
  4472	    finished.  The worker therefore reports the real state of its
  4473	    operation, the supervisor joins it with a bounded wait, and a worker
  4474	    that did not stop keeps its owner reference and makes the round
  4475	    unknown.
  4476	
  4477	    A write is a **complete** write: the endpoint writes the whole
  4478	    block, in as many partial writes as Windows needs, and it reports
  4479	    the number of the bytes that really went into the pipe.  A partial
  4480	    write is never reported as a success.
  4481	    """
  4482	
  4483	    #: The bound absolute deadline of this endpoint.  It is a class
  4484	    #: attribute so that an endpoint that was built without its
  4485	    #: constructor still has a real deadline and never a missing one.
  4486	    _deadline = None
  4487	    #: The cancel flag and the deadline of the write that is in flight.
  4488	    #: They are class attributes for the same reason as the deadline
  4489	    #: above: an endpoint that was built without its constructor still
  4490	    #: has them and never a missing one.
  4491	    _write_cancel = None
  4492	    _write_deadline = None
  4493	    #: The context of the round.  It is a class attribute for the same
  4494	    #: reason: an endpoint that was built without its constructor still
  4495	    #: reads the absolute deadline of the round from it and it never
  4496	    #: treats a missing context as an unlimited budget.
  4497	    _context = None
  4498	    #: The lock of the **admission** of the endpoint.  It is a class
  4499	    #: attribute for the same reason: an endpoint that was built without
  4500	    #: its constructor still has a **real** admission and it never has a
  4501	    #: permissive one.  The lock is created **per instance** on its
